② The marsh is full of sedges and reeds that turn brown in winter.
= 那片沼泽长满了莎草和芦苇，冬天都变成褐色。
sedges and reeds = 莎草和芦苇

sediment
/ˈsedɪmənt/
核心：名词，不可数或可数：沉淀物、沉积物：（液体底部的）渣滓；（地质）（河流、海洋带来的）泥沙沉积。
① There was a layer of brown sediment at the bottom of the old wine bottle.
= 那瓶陈年葡萄酒的瓶底有一层褐色的沉淀物。
a layer of sediment = 一层沉淀物
sediment = 沉淀物
② The river carries tons of sediment down from the mountains every year.
= 那条河每年从山上冲下数吨泥沙。
carry sediment = 携带泥沙

sedimentary
/ˌsedɪˈmentri/
核心：形容词（地质）：沉积的、沉积而成的：sedimentary rock。
① Our teacher explained that sandstone and limestone are sedimentary rocks formed over millions of years.
= 老师解释说，砂岩和石灰岩是经过几百万年形成的沉积岩。
sedimentary rocks = 沉积岩
sedimentary = 沉积的
② The cliffs along the coast show layers of sedimentary rock in many different colours.
= 沿岸的悬崖呈现出许多不同颜色的沉积岩层。
layers of sedimentary rock = 沉积岩层

sedimentation
/ˌsedɪmenˈteɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：沉积（作用）、沉淀（过程）。
① Sedimentation has made the harbour so shallow that big ships can no longer use it.
= 泥沙沉积让港口变得太浅，大船已经无法使用了。
sedimentation = 沉积
② The water is cleaned by sedimentation before it is filtered and sent to homes.
= 水先经过沉淀处理，再过滤后送到各家各户。
clean water by sedimentation = 用沉淀法净化水

sedition
/sɪˈdɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（正式）：煽动叛乱（的言行）：煽动人们反抗政府的言论或文字。形容词 seditious 另立条。
① The journalist was arrested and charged with sedition after he wrote an article attacking the king.
= 那位记者写了一篇抨击国王的文章，随后被捕，被控煽动叛乱。
be charged with sedition = 被控煽动叛乱
sedition = 煽动叛乱
② Under the old laws, criticizing the government in public could be treated as sedition.
= 在旧法律下，公开批评政府可能被当作煽动叛乱。
be treated as sedition = 被当作煽动叛乱

seditious
/sɪˈdɪʃəs/
核心：形容词（正式）：煽动叛乱的、煽动性的：seditious speeches。
① The police seized thousands of seditious leaflets calling on soldiers to refuse orders.
= 警方查获了数千份号召士兵抗命的煽动性传单。
seditious leaflets = 煽动性传单
seditious = 煽动叛乱的
② The poet was sent to prison for writing seditious songs about the president.
= 那位诗人因写了关于总统的煽动性歌曲而入狱。
seditious songs = 煽动性歌曲

seduce
/sɪˈdjuːs/
核心：动词，及物：引诱、勾引（与之发生性关系）；（比喻）诱使、吸引：be seduced by something、seduce somebody into doing something。名词 seduction，人是 seducer / seductress，形容词 seductive（均另立条）。
① In the novel, the rich young man seduces a poor village girl and then leaves her.
= 那部小说里，那个有钱的年轻人勾引了一个贫穷的村姑，然后抛弃了她。
seduce somebody = 勾引某人
seduce = 引诱、勾引
② Many young people are seduced by the idea of becoming rich and famous overnight.
= 许多年轻人被一夜成名致富的念头所吸引。
be seduced by something = 被某事吸引
③ The low prices seduced thousands of customers into buying things they did not need.
= 低价诱使成千上万的顾客买了他们并不需要的东西。
seduce somebody into doing something = 诱使某人做某事

seducer
/sɪˈdjuːsə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：引诱者、勾引者（尤指男子）。
① The handsome seducer in the opera promises to marry every woman he meets.
= 那部歌剧里英俊的勾引者对遇到的每个女人都许诺要娶她。
a seducer = 勾引者
② He was known in the town as a seducer of other men's wives.
= 他在镇上出了名的专勾引别人的老婆。
a seducer of somebody = 勾引某人的人

seduction
/sɪˈdʌkʃn/
核心：名词：引诱、勾引（不可数或可数）；（常复数）诱惑力、魅力：the seductions of city life。
① The film tells the story of the seduction of a young nun by a rich lord.
= 那部电影讲的是一个有钱的贵族勾引一个年轻修女的故事。
the seduction of somebody = 对某人的勾引
seduction = 勾引
② She could not resist the seductions of life in Paris, with its cafés, music and fashion.
= 巴黎生活的魅力让她难以抗拒：咖啡馆、音乐和时装。
the seductions of something = 某物的诱惑力

seductive
/sɪˈdʌktɪv/
核心：形容词：性感的、有诱惑力的：a seductive voice；诱人的、有吸引力的：a seductive idea。副词 seductively 是诱人地、妩媚地。名词 seductiveness 另立条。
① She spoke in a soft, seductive voice that made every man in the room turn round.
= 她用一种温柔性感的嗓音说话，屋里每个男人都转过头来。
a seductive voice = 性感的嗓音
seductive = 性感的、诱人的
② The idea of working only four days a week is very seductive, but it is not always practical.
= 一周只工作四天的想法很诱人，但并不总是可行。
seductive = 诱人的
③ The singer smiled seductively at the camera at the end of the video.
= 音乐录像结尾，那位歌手对着镜头妩媚地一笑。
seductively = 妩媚地

seductiveness
/sɪˈdʌktɪvnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：诱惑力、魅力。
① Clever advertisers understand very well the seductiveness of words like "new" and "free".
= 精明的广告商非常清楚「新」和「免费」这类词的诱惑力。
the seductiveness of something = 某物的诱惑力
seductiveness = 诱惑力
② Part of the seductiveness of the island is that there are no cars and no phones.
= 那座岛的魅力部分在于那里没有汽车，也没有电话。
part of the seductiveness = 魅力的一部分

seductress
/sɪˈdʌktrəs/
核心：名词，可数：（勾引男人的）女人、妖妇。多见于小说、电影。
① In the old film noir, the hero is ruined by a beautiful seductress who wants his money.
= 那部老黑色电影里，主角被一个贪图他钱财的美艳妖妇毁了。
a seductress = 妖妇
② She played the role of the seductress in a famous opera at the age of twenty-five.
= 她二十五岁时在一部著名歌剧中饰演那个妖妇角色。
play the seductress = 饰演妖妇

sedulous
/ˈsedjələs/
核心：形容词（正式）：勤勉的、孜孜不倦的、一丝不苟的。副词 sedulously 是勤勉地、刻意地。
① She was a sedulous student who spent every evening in the library.
= 她是个勤勉的学生，每天晚上都待在图书馆。
a sedulous student = 勤勉的学生
sedulous = 勤勉的
② During the long interview, the minister sedulously avoided any questions about his private life.
= 在那次长时间的采访中，那位部长刻意回避任何关于他私生活的问题。
sedulously avoid something = 刻意回避某事

see
/siː/
核心：动词（过去式 saw，过去分词 seen），本义是「眼睛看见」，由此长出一大族：
看见：不是主动去看，是眼睛接收到，和 look、watch 不同；（电影、比赛）看；见面、会见：see a doctor；明白、理解：I see；认为、看待：see something as；发现、得知：I see that…；查看、弄清：see if / whether；确保、注意：see that…；陪伴、送：see somebody home；经历、目睹（时代、事件）：the year saw…；交往、谈恋爱：be seeing somebody。
习语：see you 是再见；let me see 是让我想想；you see 是你知道、你明白吧；wait and see 是等着瞧；see eye to eye 是看法一致；see red 是大怒；as far as I can see 是依我看。
短语动词：see about 是安排、处理；see in 是迎接（新年）；see off 是送行，也指击退；see out 是送……出门，也指坚持到底；see over 是察看（房子）；see through 是看穿，也指坚持到底；see to 是照料、处理；see to it that 是确保。复合词 see-saw（也作 seesaw）是跷跷板，比喻起伏不定。
① When I open the kitchen curtains in the morning, I often see a fox in the garden.
= 我早上拉开厨房窗帘时，常常看见花园里有一只狐狸。
see = 看见（不是主动去看，是眼睛接收到）
② Have you seen the new film about the moon landing at the cinema in town yet?
= 你在镇上的电影院看过那部关于登月的新电影了吗？
see a film = 看电影
seen = 看（see 的过去分词）
③ You should see a doctor about that cough; it has lasted for three weeks.
= 你那咳嗽该去看看医生了，已经三个星期了。
see a doctor = 看医生
④ "You have to press this button first." "Oh, I see. Thank you."
= 「你得先按这个按钮。」「哦，我明白了，谢谢。」
see = 明白
口语里 I see 是「我明白了」，用一般现在时，不说 I am seeing。
⑤ Many people see the new law as an attack on their freedom.
= 许多人把那项新法律看作对他们自由的侵犯。
see something as something = 把某事看作某事
⑥ Can you see if there is any milk left in the fridge?
= 你能看看冰箱里还有没有牛奶吗？
see if = 看看是否
⑦ Please see that all the windows are closed before you leave the office.
= 离开办公室之前，请确保所有的窗户都关好了。
see that something is done = 留意把某事办好
⑧ It is late, so I will see you home and then walk back to the station.
= 天晚了，我送你回家，然后再走回车站。
see somebody home = 送某人回家
⑨ The last century has seen huge changes in the way people travel, work and talk to each other.
= 上个世纪，人们的出行、工作和交流方式都发生了巨大变化。
a period sees something = 某时期见证了某事
⑩ She has been seeing a doctor from the hospital for about six months.
= 她和医院的一位医生交往了大约六个月。
be seeing somebody = 和某人交往
⑪ "See you tomorrow at school!" she called to her friend as she ran for the bus.
= 「明天学校见！」她冲朋友喊着，跑去赶公共汽车。
see you = 再见
⑫ "How old is your car?" "Let me see — I bought it in 2015, so it is ten years old."
= 「你的车多少年了？」「让我想想——我二〇一五年买的，所以有十年了。」
let me see = 让我想想
⑬ I couldn't come to the party, you see, because my mother was ill.
= 你知道，我没法来参加聚会，因为我妈病了。
you see = 你知道、你明白吧
⑭ I don't know if they will give me the job; we will just have to wait and see.
= 我不知道他们会不会给我那份工作，只能等着瞧了。
wait and see = 等着瞧
⑮ My brother and I don't always see eye to eye about politics.
= 我和哥哥在政治上并不总是看法一致。
see eye to eye = 看法一致
⑯ My father sees red whenever he finds that someone has parked in front of his garage.
= 每当发现有人把车停在他车库门口，我父亲就勃然大怒。
see red = 大怒
⑰ As far as I can see, there is nothing wrong with the engine.
= 依我看，发动机没什么毛病。
as far as I can see = 依我看
⑱ Don't worry about the train tickets; I'll see about them first thing tomorrow morning.
= 别担心火车票的事，我明天一早就去处理。
see about something = 处理某事
⑲ We are going to see in the New Year at a party with all our neighbours.
= 我们打算和所有邻居一起在一个聚会上迎接新年。
see in = 迎接（新年）
⑳ When she left for Australia, the whole family came to the airport to see her off.
= 她去澳大利亚时，全家人都到机场为她送行。
see somebody off = 为某人送行
多在车站、机场，宾语是人，代词要放在 see 和 off 中间。
㉑ My secretary will see you out when you are ready to leave.
= 你准备走的时候，我的秘书会送你出去。
see somebody out = 送某人出门
㉒ An agent will meet you at the house so that you can see over it before you decide.
= 中介会在那座房子跟你碰头，让你先看看房再决定。
see over something = 察看某处
㉓ She can see through his lies at once, so she refuses to lend him any more money.
= 她一眼就能看穿他的谎言，所以不肯再借钱给他。
see through somebody = 看穿某人
㉔ Can you see to the children's dinner while I take the dog for a walk?
= 我去遛狗的时候，你能照料一下孩子们的晚饭吗？
see to something = 照料、处理某事
㉕ The manager will see to it that you are paid by the end of the week.
= 经理会确保你在周末前拿到工钱。
see to it that = 确保
㉖ The children took turns on the see-saw in the park until it got dark.
= 孩子们在公园里轮流玩跷跷板，一直玩到天黑。
a see-saw = 跷跷板

seed
/siːd/
核心：名词：种子、籽（可数或不可数）：plant seeds、sunflower seeds；（比喻）根源、开端：the seeds of something；（体育）种子选手：the top seed。动词：播种；（植物）结籽；（体育）把……列为种子选手：be seeded；去籽。
习语：go to seed / run to seed 是（植物）结籽而不再长叶，比喻（人、地方）走下坡路、衰败。
复合词：seed money 是启动资金；seed cake 是香籽蛋糕（加葛缕子籽的蛋糕）；seed coat 是种皮；seed corn 是留作种子的谷物，比喻为将来所做的投资；seed pearl 是小粒珍珠。
① Plant the seeds in small pots and keep them in a warm place until they start to grow.
= 把种子种在小花盆里，放在温暖的地方，直到发芽。
seed = 种子
plant seeds = 播种
② Every winter morning the children fed sunflower seeds to the birds in the garden.
= 冬天每天早上，孩子们都在花园里用葵花籽喂鸟。
sunflower seed = 葵花籽
③ The seeds of the war were sown many years before the first shot was fired.
= 那场战争的根源早在第一枪打响的许多年前就埋下了。
the seeds of something = 某事的根源
④ After he lost his job, he let himself go to seed and stopped looking after his house.
= 他失业后就开始颓废，不再打理房子。
go to seed = 走下坡路
⑤ The young company needs seed money from investors to build its first product.
= 那家初创公司需要投资人的启动资金来做出第一款产品。
seed money = 启动资金
⑥ She was the top seed in the tennis tournament but lost in the second round.
= 她是那次网球锦标赛的头号种子，却在第二轮就输了。
the top seed = 头号种子
⑦ My grandmother always made a seed cake with caraway seeds for Sunday tea.
= 我祖母星期天喝下午茶时总要做一个加葛缕子籽的香籽蛋糕。
a seed cake = 香籽蛋糕
⑧ The hard seed coat protects the seed until it is planted in wet soil.
= 坚硬的种皮保护着种子，直到它被种进湿润的土里。
the seed coat = 种皮
⑨ Cutting money for schools is like eating your seed corn; it saves money now but hurts the future.
= 削减学校经费就像把留种的粮食吃掉，眼下省了钱，却损害了将来。
seed corn = 留种的谷物；为将来的投资
⑩ My grandmother's old necklace was made of hundreds of tiny seed pearls.
= 我祖母那条旧项链是用几百颗小粒珍珠串成的。
a seed pearl = 小粒珍珠

seedbed
/ˈsiːdbed/
核心：名词，可数：苗床：为播种准备的细土地块；（比喻）（思想、人才等的）温床、发源地。
① Rake the soil until it is fine and flat to make a good seedbed for the carrots.
= 把土耙细耙平，给胡萝卜做一块好苗床。
a seedbed = 苗床
② The small university became a seedbed for new ideas in science and art.
= 那所小大学成了科学和艺术新思想的温床。
a seedbed for something = 某物的温床

seeded
/ˈsiːdɪd/
核心：形容词：（体育）被列为种子的：the third seeded player；去了籽的；（面包）撒了籽的。
① The third seeded player lost to a young unknown player in the first round.
= 三号种子选手在第一轮输给了一个年轻的无名选手。
the third seeded player = 三号种子选手
seeded = 被列为种子的
② She bought a loaf of seeded bread and some cheese for lunch.
= 她买了一条撒籽面包和一些奶酪当午饭。
seeded bread = 撒籽面包

seediness
/ˈsiːdinəs/
核心：名词，不可数：（地方的）破败、肮脏下流。由 seedy 变 y 为 i 加 -ness 构成。
① The seediness of the area around the old station surprised the tourists who arrived at night.
= 夜里到达的游客对老车站周围一带的破败感到吃惊。
the seediness of a place = 某地的破败
seediness = 破败
② Despite its seediness, the old hotel was full of famous writers in the 1950s.
= 那家老旅馆虽然破败，二十世纪五十年代却住满了名作家。
despite its seediness = 虽然破败

seedless
/ˈsiːdləs/
核心：形容词：（水果）无籽的：seedless grapes。
① The children prefer seedless grapes because they don't have to spit anything out.
= 孩子们更喜欢无籽葡萄，因为不用吐籽。
seedless grapes = 无籽葡萄
seedless = 无籽的
② Most of the watermelons sold in the big supermarkets are now seedless and quite small.
= 现在大超市里卖的西瓜大多是无籽的，个头也挺小。
be seedless = 是无籽的

seedling
/ˈsiːdlɪŋ/
核心：名词，可数：幼苗、秧苗：刚从种子长出来的小植物。
① We planted the tomato seedlings in the garden as soon as the weather was warm enough.
= 天气一暖和，我们就把番茄苗种到了园子里。
seedlings = 幼苗
② Slugs ate half of our young lettuce seedlings in a single night.
= 鼻涕虫一夜之间吃掉了我们一半的生菜幼苗。
lettuce seedlings = 生菜幼苗

seedsman
/ˈsiːdzmən/
核心：名词，可数，复数 seedsmen：种子商、卖种子的人。
① The seedsman in the market sold us packets of seeds for beans, carrots and peas.
= 集市上的种子商卖给我们几包豆子、胡萝卜和豌豆的种子。
a seedsman = 种子商
② My great-grandfather was a seedsman who travelled from farm to farm with his cart.
= 我曾祖父是个种子商，赶着马车一个农场一个农场地跑。
work as a seedsman = 当种子商

seedy
/ˈsiːdi/
核心：形容词：（地方）破败肮脏的、下流的、名声不好的：a seedy bar；（旧式口语）不舒服的。比较级 seedier。名词 seediness 另立条。
① The detective went into a seedy bar near the docks to look for the missing man.
= 侦探走进码头附近一家下三滥的酒吧，寻找那个失踪的男人。
a seedy bar = 下三滥的酒吧
seedy = 破败肮脏的
② The film shows the seedy side of the music business, with drugs and cheating managers.
= 那部电影展现了音乐圈的阴暗面：毒品和行骗的经纪人。
the seedy side of something = 某事的阴暗面

seek
/siːk/
核心：动词（过去式和过去分词 sought），比 look for 正式：寻找、寻求：seek a job、seek shelter；请求、征求：seek advice、seek help、seek permission；试图、设法：seek to do something。复合词 job-seeker 是求职者。短语动词 seek out 是找出、搜寻到。名词 seeker（另立条）。复合词 seek time（计算机）是寻道时间。
① Thousands of people seek work in the city every year, but many of them cannot find it.
= 每年有成千上万的人到城里找工作，可其中许多人找不到。
seek = 寻求
seek work = 找工作
② If you are not sure what to do, you should seek advice from a lawyer.
= 如果你拿不准该怎么办，应该向律师求教。
seek advice = 求教
③ The government is seeking to reduce the number of people without homes.
= 政府正设法减少无家可归者的人数。
seek to do something = 设法做某事
④ The office helps job-seekers to write their CVs and prepare for interviews.
= 那个办事处帮求职者写简历、准备面试。
job-seeker = 求职者
⑤ When the storm began, we sought shelter in a little church by the road.
= 暴风雨来时，我们在路边一座小教堂里躲雨。
seek shelter = 寻找躲避的地方
⑥ The young writer sought out the old poet and asked him to read her poems.
= 那位年轻作家专门找到了那位老诗人，请他读她的诗。
seek out = 找出、专门找到
⑦ A faster hard drive has a shorter seek time, so files open more quickly.
= 硬盘越快，寻道时间越短，文件打开得也越快。
seek time = （计算机）寻道时间

seeker
/ˈsiːkə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：寻求者、追求者：多构成复合词：asylum seeker（寻求庇护者）、job-seeker（求职者）、attention seeker（爱出风头的人）。
① The country has received thousands of asylum seekers from the war in the north.
= 那个国家接收了成千上万从北方战火中逃来的寻求庇护者。
asylum seekers = 寻求庇护者
a seeker = 寻求者
② He is a seeker of truth who has studied every religion in the world.
= 他是个追求真理的人，研究过世界上的每一种宗教。
a seeker of truth = 追求真理的人

seem
/siːm/
核心：动词，连系动词，不用进行时：似乎、看起来、好像：seem + 形容词：seem happy；seem to do / to be something；It seems that…、It seems as if…；（表示说话人的印象）I seem to have lost my keys。比 look 更侧重主观判断，不只看外表。形容词 seeming（另立条）是表面上的。
① She seems very happy in her new job, and she has made a lot of friends there.
= 她在新工作里似乎很开心，在那儿交了很多朋友。
seem + 形容词 = 看起来…
② It seems that nobody knows where the old key is, so we will have to change the lock.
= 好像谁都不知道那把旧钥匙在哪儿，所以我们只好换锁。
It seems that... = 看起来…
也说 It seems to me that…，语气更委婉，表示说话人自己的看法。
③ The children seem to enjoy the new teacher's lessons much more than the old ones.
= 孩子们似乎喜欢新老师的课多过以前的。
seem to do = 似乎…
④ I seem to have left my umbrella on the train this morning.
= 我今天早上好像把伞落在火车上了。
I seem to have done something = 我好像做了某事
⑤ It seemed as if the rain would never stop, but the sun came out in the afternoon.
= 雨好像永远不会停，可下午太阳出来了。
It seems as if = 好像
⑥ He is not as stupid as he seems; he just doesn't like talking in front of people.
= 他并不像看上去那么笨，只是不喜欢在人前讲话。
as somebody seems = 像某人看上去那样

seeming
/ˈsiːmɪŋ/
核心：形容词，只作定语（正式）：表面上的、似乎……的：her seeming calm。副词 seemingly 是表面上、看似；（据说）显然。
① Despite her seeming calm, she was very nervous before the job interview that morning.
= 尽管她表面上很镇定，那天早上面试前其实非常紧张。
seeming = 表面上的
her seeming calm = 她表面上的镇定
② All week the workers were busy with a seemingly endless list of jobs on the old house.
= 一整个星期，工人们都在那座老房子上忙着一长串看似没完没了的活儿。
seemingly = 看似
③ Seemingly, the old house has been sold to a family from London.
= 据说那座老房子已经卖给了一户伦敦人家。
seemingly = 据说、显然

seemly
/ˈsiːmli/
核心：形容词（旧式）：（行为）得体的、合乎礼仪的。反义 unseemly 更常用。比较级 seemlier。
① In those days it was not considered seemly for a young woman to go to the theatre alone.
= 在那个年代，年轻女子独自去剧院被认为不合礼仪。
seemly = 得体的
② He thought it was more seemly to wait a year before he married again.
= 他觉得再等一年再婚更得体些。
more seemly = 更得体

seep
/siːp/
核心：动词，不及物：（液体、气体）渗出、渗漏：seep into / out of / through something；（比喻）（思想、情绪）慢慢渗透、扩散。
① Water seeped through the cracks in the roof and dripped onto the bedroom floor.
= 水从屋顶的裂缝渗进来，滴到卧室的地板上。
seep through something = 从某处渗出
seep = 渗出、渗漏
② Slowly, the news of the factory closing began to seep into the town.
= 工厂倒闭的消息慢慢在镇上传开了。
seep into something = 慢慢渗入某处

seepage
/ˈsiːpɪdʒ/
核心：名词，不可数：渗漏；渗出的液体。
① The engineers found that seepage from the old pipes was making the walls damp.
= 工程师发现，是旧管道的渗漏让墙体变潮了。
seepage = 渗漏
② There was a slow seepage of oil from the bottom of the car onto the garage floor.
= 汽车底下有机油缓缓渗出，滴到了车库地板上。
a seepage of oil = 漏油

seer
/sɪə(r)/
核心：名词，可数（文学）：预言家、先知：能预见未来的人。
① The old king asked a seer to tell him whether he would win the battle.
= 老国王请一位预言家告诉他能否打赢那场仗。
a seer = 预言家
② Many people treated the economist as a seer after he predicted the crisis.
= 那位经济学家预测到危机之后，许多人把他当成了先知。
treat somebody as a seer = 把某人当先知

seersucker
/ˈsɪəsʌkə(r)/
核心：名词，不可数：泡泡纱：一种表面起皱、通常带条纹的轻薄棉布，常做夏装。
① He wore a blue and white seersucker jacket to the garden party on a hot afternoon.
= 炎热的下午，他穿着一件蓝白条纹泡泡纱外套去参加花园聚会。
a seersucker jacket = 泡泡纱外套
seersucker = 泡泡纱
② Seersucker is popular in the American South because it keeps you cool in summer.
= 泡泡纱在美国南方很受欢迎，因为夏天穿着凉快。
seersucker is popular = 泡泡纱受欢迎

seethe
/siːð/
核心：动词，不及物：（内心）怒火中烧、生闷气：seethe with anger；（地方）挤满、充斥（人或活动）：seethe with people；（液体）翻滚、沸腾。
① She was seething with anger after her boss criticized her in front of the whole team.
= 老板当着全组的面批评了她，她一肚子火。
seethe with anger = 怒火中烧
seethe = 生闷气
② On market day, the little square seethes with people buying fruit and vegetables.
= 赶集那天，小广场上挤满了买水果蔬菜的人。
seethe with people = 挤满了人

segment
/ˈseɡmənt/（名）/seɡˈment/（动）
核心：名词，可数：部分、片段、段：a segment of the population；（橘子等的）瓣：orange segments；（数学）（圆的）弓形、线段。动词，及物：把……分割、分成若干部分：segment the market。
① Older people are the fastest-growing segment of the population in many countries.
= 在许多国家，老年人是人口中增长最快的部分。
a segment of the population = 人口中的一部分
a segment = 部分
② She peeled the orange and gave each of the children three segments.
= 她剥了橘子，给每个孩子三瓣。
orange segments = 橘子瓣
③ The company segments its customers by age and income and sends them different offers.
= 那家公司按年龄和收入给顾客分组，给他们发送不同的优惠。
segment customers = 给顾客分组

segmental
/seɡˈmentl/
核心：形容词：部分的、片段的；（语言学）音段的。
① The report gives a segmental analysis of the market, looking at each group of customers in turn.
= 那份报告对市场做了分块分析，依次考察每一组顾客。
a segmental analysis = 分块分析
segmental = 分块的
② The little worm in the garden has a segmental body made of many small rings.
= 花园里那种小虫子的身体由许多小环节组成，是分节的。
a segmental body = 分节的身体

segmentation
/ˌseɡmenˈteɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数或可数：分割、划分；（市场）细分：market segmentation。
① Market segmentation helps the company decide which products to sell to which customers.
= 市场细分帮助那家公司决定把哪些产品卖给哪些顾客。
market segmentation = 市场细分
segmentation = 划分
② The segmentation of the city into rich and poor areas has grown worse over the years.
= 这些年来，城市按贫富划分区域的现象越来越严重了。
the segmentation of something = 某物的划分

segregate
/ˈseɡrɪɡeɪt/
核心：动词，及物：（按种族、宗教、性别）隔离、分开：be segregated by race；把……分开：segregate something from something。名词 segregation。
① In some countries, schools used to be segregated by race, and black and white children could not study together.
= 在一些国家，学校曾按种族隔离，黑人孩子和白人孩子不能一起上学。
be segregated by race = 按种族隔离
segregate = 隔离
② During the winter, the hospital segregates patients with the virus from the other patients.
= 冬天里，那家医院把感染病毒的病人和其他病人隔开。
segregate somebody from somebody = 把某人与某人隔开

segregation
/ˌseɡrɪˈɡeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：（种族、宗教、性别的）隔离（政策）：racial segregation；分开、分离。
① The civil rights movement fought to end racial segregation in the southern states.
= 民权运动为结束南方各州的种族隔离而斗争。
racial segregation = 种族隔离
segregation = 隔离
② The segregation of boys and girls in the school ended in the 1970s.
= 那所学校男女分校的做法在二十世纪七十年代结束了。
the segregation of somebody = 把某人分开

segregationist
/ˌseɡrɪˈɡeɪʃənɪst/
核心：名词，可数：种族隔离主义者。形容词：主张种族隔离的。
① The governor was a segregationist who fought to keep black children out of white schools.
= 那位州长是个种族隔离主义者，竭力不让黑人孩子进白人学校。
a segregationist = 种族隔离主义者
② In its first year, the new government repealed all the old segregationist laws of the state.
= 新政府上台第一年就废除了该州所有旧的种族隔离法律。
segregationist laws = 种族隔离法律

segue
/ˈseɡweɪ/
核心：动词，不及物：（音乐、话题）顺利转入、自然过渡：segue into something。名词，可数：（顺畅的）过渡、转换。来自意大利语。
① The band finished one song and segued straight into the next without stopping.
= 乐队唱完一首歌，不停顿地直接转入下一首。
segue into something = 顺利转入某事
segue = 自然过渡
② The presenter made a clever segue from the weather to a story about summer holidays.
= 主持人巧妙地从天气过渡到了一个关于暑假的故事。
a segue from A to B = 从 A 到 B 的过渡

seine
/seɪn/
核心：名词，可数：围网、拖网：渔民围捕鱼群用的大网。动词：用围网捕鱼。
① The fishermen pulled the heavy seine onto the beach, full of silver fish.
= 渔民们把装满银色鱼儿的沉重围网拖上了海滩。
a seine = 围网
② In the old days the villagers seined for fish in the river every spring.
= 从前，村民们每年春天都在河里用围网捕鱼。
seine for fish = 用围网捕鱼

seismic
/ˈsaɪzmɪk/
核心：形容词：地震的、地震引起的：seismic activity、seismic waves；（比喻）巨大的、影响深远的：a seismic shift。
① Scientists have recorded increased seismic activity under the volcano this week, so the villages are on alert.
= 科学家记录到本周火山下方的地震活动有所增强，所以附近村庄都处于戒备状态。
seismic activity = 地震活动
seismic = 地震的
② The invention of the internet caused a seismic shift in the way people work and shop.
= 互联网的发明使人们的工作和购物方式发生了巨大转变。
a seismic shift = 巨大转变

seismograph
/ˈsaɪzməɡrɑːf/
核心：名词，可数：地震仪、地震检波器：记录地面震动的仪器。
① The seismograph at the university recorded the earthquake even though it was five hundred miles away.
= 尽管地震发生在五百英里以外，大学的地震仪还是记录到了。
a seismograph = 地震仪
② The needle of the seismograph moved wildly as the ground began to shake.
= 地面开始震动时，地震仪的指针剧烈摆动。
the needle of a seismograph = 地震仪的指针

seismological
/ˌsaɪzməˈlɒdʒɪkl/
核心：形容词：地震学的。
① The national seismological centre warned people on the coast about a possible tsunami.
= 国家地震中心警告沿海居民可能发生海啸。
a seismological centre = 地震中心
seismological = 地震学的
② The team collected seismological data from hundreds of stations around the world.
= 那个团队收集了全球数百个台站的地震学数据。
seismological data = 地震学数据

seismologist
/saɪzˈmɒlədʒɪst/
核心：名词，可数：地震学家。
① Seismologists say that a large earthquake could hit the city at any time in the next fifty years.
= 地震学家说，未来五十年里那座城市随时可能发生大地震。
seismologists = 地震学家
② The seismologist explained how the movement of the rocks deep underground causes earthquakes.
= 那位地震学家解释了地下深处岩石的移动是如何引发地震的。
a seismologist = 地震学家

seismology
/saɪzˈmɒlədʒi/
核心：名词，不可数：地震学：研究地震及地球内部震动的科学。
① She studied seismology at university because she grew up in a city that often had earthquakes.
= 她在大学学地震学，因为她从小生活在一座经常发生地震的城市。
study seismology = 学地震学
seismology = 地震学
② Advances in seismology have made it easier to build houses that can survive earthquakes.
= 地震学的进步让人们更容易建造能抗震的房屋。
advances in seismology = 地震学的进步

seize
/siːz/
核心：动词：（突然用力）抓住、夺取：seize somebody by the arm；（武力）夺取、占领：seize power、seize control；（警方、海关）没收、查获：seize drugs；抓住（机会）：seize the opportunity；（情绪）突然侵袭：be seized by panic。和 hold 的分别：hold 是持续地握着，seize 是一下子用力抓住。
习语：seize the day 是及时行乐、抓住今天。短语动词：seize on / seize upon 是抓住（机会、借口、错误）不放；seize up 是（机器、肌肉）卡住、僵住。名词 seizure 另立条。
① The man seized her bag and ran off down the street before she could shout.
= 那个男人一把抓过她的包，没等她喊出声就顺着街跑掉了。
seize = 抓住
seize = 突然用力抓住
近义对照：
hold = 握住（持续）
② The army seized power in the middle of the night and closed the airport.
= 军队在半夜夺取了政权，关闭了机场。
seize power = 夺取政权
③ Customs officers seized a large amount of drugs hidden in the back of a lorry.
= 海关人员查获了藏在一辆卡车后部的大量毒品。
seize drugs = 查获毒品
④ When she was offered a job in Paris, she seized the opportunity at once.
= 有人给她提供一份在巴黎的工作，她马上抓住了这个机会。
seize the opportunity = 抓住机会
⑤ Don't wait until you retire to travel; seize the day and go now.
= 别等退休了才去旅行，抓住今天，现在就去。
seize the day = 及时行乐
⑥ The newspapers seized on the minister's mistake and wrote about it for a week.
= 报纸抓住那位部长的失误不放，连写了一个星期。
seize on something = 抓住某事不放
⑦ The opposition seized upon the figures as proof that the government had failed.
= 反对党抓住那些数字，把它们当作政府失败的证据。
seize upon something = 抓住某事（作为把柄）
⑧ The old engine seized up halfway up the hill, and we had to call for help.
= 那台旧发动机爬坡爬到一半卡死了，我们只好打电话求救。
seize up = （机器、肌肉）卡住、僵住

seizure
/ˈsiːʒə(r)/
核心：名词：（权力、领土的）夺取（不可数或可数）：the seizure of power；（警方、海关的）没收、查获（可数或不可数）；（医学）（疾病的）突然发作、癫痫发作（可数）。
① The army's seizure of power was condemned by governments all over the world.
= 军队夺取政权受到世界各国政府的谴责。
the seizure of power = 夺取政权
seizure = 夺取
② Last month police at the port made the biggest seizure of drugs in the country's history.
= 上个月港口警方破获了该国历史上最大的一宗毒品案。
a seizure of drugs = 一宗毒品查获
③ The boy had a seizure in the classroom, and the teacher called an ambulance.
= 那个男孩在教室里突然发病，老师叫了救护车。
have a seizure = 突然发病

seldom
/ˈseldəm/
核心：副词：很少、不常：比 rarely 更书面，放在实义动词前、be 动词后。seldom if ever 是极少、几乎从不。
① My grandfather seldom watches television; he prefers to read or work in the garden.
= 我祖父很少看电视，他更喜欢看书或在花园里干活。
seldom = 很少
② The road is seldom used now that the new motorway has been built.
= 新高速公路建成后，那条路就很少有人走了。
be seldom used = 很少使用
③ She seldom if ever forgets a birthday, so she was upset when she forgot mine.
= 她几乎从不忘记谁的生日，所以忘了我的生日她很难过。
seldom if ever = 几乎从不

select
/sɪˈlekt/
核心：动词：（仔细地）挑选、选拔：select somebody for something、select from。和 choose、pick 的分别：choose 是通用的「选择」，pick 是口语的「挑」，select 正式，强调精挑细选。形容词：精选的、高级的、只供少数人的：a select group。名词 selection、形容词 selective 另立条。
① She was selected to play for the national team at the age of seventeen.
= 她十七岁就入选国家队。
select = 精挑细选（正式）
be selected for something = 入选某事
近义对照：
choose = 选择（通用）
pick = 挑（口语）
② Select a file from the list and click on the button to open it.
= 从列表中选择一个文件，点击按钮打开它。
select = 选择
③ Only a select group of people were invited to the private dinner with the prince.
= 只有少数精选的人受邀与王子共进私人晚宴。
a select group = 少数精选的人

selection
/sɪˈlekʃn/
核心：名词：选择、挑选（不可数或可数）：the selection of players；（可数）选出的人或物、精选品：a selection of cheeses；（商店的）可选货品、品种：a wide selection。复合词 selection committee 是遴选委员会。
① The selection of the new team captain took the coach several weeks.
= 挑选新队长花了教练好几个星期。
selection = 选择、精选
② The restaurant offers a selection of local cheeses after the main course.
= 那家餐馆在主菜之后提供一份当地奶酪拼盘。
a selection of something = 一组精选的某物
③ The shop has a wide selection of shoes at very reasonable prices.
= 那家店的鞋品种很多，价格也很合理。
a wide selection = 品种繁多
④ The selection committee interviewed six candidates for the job of head teacher.
= 遴选委员会面试了六名校长职位的候选人。
a selection committee = 遴选委员会

selective
/sɪˈlektɪv/
核心：形容词：选择性的、有选择的：selective schools；（人）挑剔的、精心挑选的：be selective about something。副词 selectively 是有选择地。复合词 selective service（美国）是义务兵役制。名词 selectivity 另立条。
① She is very selective about the films she watches and only goes to see a few each year.
= 她看电影很挑剔，每年只看几部。
be selective about something = 对某事很挑剔
selective = 有选择性的
② The newspaper reported the facts selectively, leaving out anything that did not support its story.
= 那家报纸有选择地报道事实，凡是不支持它说法的都略掉了。
selectively = 有选择地
③ In the 1960s, young American men had to register for selective service when they turned eighteen.
= 二十世纪六十年代，美国青年男子年满十八岁都得登记服义务兵役。
selective service = （美国）义务兵役制

selectivity
/sɪˌlekˈtɪvəti/
核心：名词，不可数：选择性；（学校的）择优录取。
① The school is famous for its selectivity and only accepts one in ten students who apply.
= 那所学校以择优录取出名，申请的学生里只收十分之一。
selectivity = 择优录取
② The selectivity of the new medicine means it attacks the disease without harming healthy cells.
= 那种新药的选择性意味着它只攻击病灶，不伤害健康细胞。
the selectivity of something = 某物的选择性

selector
/sɪˈlektə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：（英式）（运动队的）选拔委员；（机器上的）选择器、选择开关。
① The selectors left the young player out of the team for the World Cup.
= 选拔委员把那名年轻球员排除在世界杯阵容之外。
the selectors = 选拔委员
② Turn the selector on the washing machine to the cold wash setting.
= 把洗衣机的选择开关转到冷水洗档。
the selector = 选择开关

selenium
/səˈliːniəm/
核心：名词，不可数（化学）：硒：一种化学元素，人体必需的微量元素，也用于太阳能电池。
① Brazil nuts are rich in selenium, which the body needs in very small amounts.
= 巴西坚果富含硒，人体对它的需求量很小。
selenium = 硒
② Some farmers add selenium to the soil because the local crops contain too little of it.
= 有些农民往土里加硒，因为当地庄稼含硒太少。
add selenium = 加硒

self
/self/
核心：名词，可数或不可数，复数 selves：自我、自身、本性：your true self、your old self；（心理学）自我意识。常说 be your old self again（恢复常态）、a sense of self（自我认同感）。作构词成分 self- 表示「自己、自动」，构成一大族复合词，多数单立。
① After the long illness, it was good to see my grandfather back to his old self, laughing and telling jokes.
= 久病之后，看到祖父恢复了往日的样子，又说又笑，真好。
your old self = 往日的样子
self = 自我
② On holiday, away from work and worry, she felt she could finally be her true self.
= 度假时远离工作和烦恼，她觉得终于可以做真实的自己了。
your true self = 真实的自我
③ Teenagers need time and space to develop a strong sense of self.
= 青少年需要时间和空间来建立强烈的自我认同感。
a sense of self = 自我认同感
④ The two sisters had very different selves at home and at school.
= 那两姐妹在家里和在学校里判若两人。
selves = 自我（复数）

self-absorbed
/ˌself əbˈzɔːbd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只顾自己的、沉溺于自我的：只关心自己的事、不在意别人。
① He was so self-absorbed that he did not notice his wife had been crying all evening.
= 他只顾着自己，竟没注意到妻子整晚都在哭。
self-absorbed = 只顾自己的
② Some young stars become self-absorbed after a few years of fame and forget their old friends.
= 有些年轻明星出名几年后就变得只顾自己，忘了老朋友。
become self-absorbed = 变得只顾自己

self-absorption
/ˌself əbˈzɔːpʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：只顾自己、沉溺于自我。
① His self-absorption made him a difficult man to live with, and his marriage soon ended.
= 他只顾自己，很难相处，婚姻很快就结束了。
self-absorption = 只顾自己
② The novel is a study of the self-absorption of a rich family that ignores the poor around them.
= 那部小说剖析了一个富裕家庭的自我沉溺，他们对身边的穷人视而不见。
the self-absorption of somebody = 某人的自我沉溺

self-abuse
/ˌself əˈbjuːs/
核心：名词，不可数：自我伤害、自残；（旧式委婉）手淫。
① The small local charity helps young people who suffer from depression and self-abuse.
= 那家当地的小慈善机构帮助患抑郁症、有自我伤害行为的年轻人。
self-abuse = 自我伤害
② By the time he was fifty, years of heavy drinking and self-abuse had damaged his health badly.
= 到五十岁时，多年酗酒和自我糟蹋已经严重损害了他的健康。
years of self-abuse = 多年的自我糟蹋

self-access
/ˌself ˈækses/
核心：名词，不可数：（学习的）自主学习、自主使用：学生自行使用学习资源。多作定语：a self-access centre。
① The language school has a self-access centre where students can study with computers and books.
= 那所语言学校有一个自主学习中心，学生可以在那里用电脑和书自学。
a self-access centre = 自主学习中心
self-access = 自主学习
② Self-access materials in the library allow learners to practise grammar at their own speed.
= 图书馆里的自主学习材料让学习者能按自己的进度练习语法。
self-access materials = 自主学习材料

self-actualization
/ˌself ˌæktʃuəlaɪˈzeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（心理学）：自我实现：充分发挥自己潜能的状态。英式也拼作 self-actualisation。
① According to the psychologist, self-actualization is the highest human need, after food, safety and love.
= 那位心理学家认为，自我实现是人类继食物、安全和爱之后的最高需求。
self-actualization = 自我实现
② For her, painting was a path to self-actualization rather than a way to earn money.
= 对她来说，画画是通往自我实现的道路，而不是谋生的手段。
a path to self-actualization = 通往自我实现的道路

self-addressed
/ˌself əˈdrest/
核心：形容词：（信封）写好寄信人自己地址的：a self-addressed envelope，常和 stamped 连用（缩写 SAE）。
① Send your application with a stamped self-addressed envelope, and we will return your photos.
= 把申请连同一个贴好邮票、写好你自己地址的信封寄来，我们会把照片退还给你。
a self-addressed envelope = 写好自己地址的回邮信封
self-addressed = 写好寄信人地址的
② Please include a self-addressed label so that we can send you the results by post.
= 请附上一张写好你地址的标签，好让我们把结果邮寄给你。
a self-addressed label = 写好自己地址的标签

self-adhesive
/ˌself ədˈhiːsɪv/
核心：形容词：自粘的、不干胶的：背面带胶、不用另外涂胶。
① We bought self-adhesive hooks so that we could hang pictures without making holes in the wall.
= 我们买了自粘挂钩，这样挂画就不用在墙上打孔了。
self-adhesive hooks = 自粘挂钩
self-adhesive = 自粘的
② The stamps are self-adhesive, so you do not need to lick them.
= 这些邮票是不干胶的，所以不用舔。
be self-adhesive = 是自粘的

self-appointed

/ˌself əˈpreɪzl/

self-assembly
/ˌself əˈsembli/

① We bought self-assembly furniture to save money, but it took us all weekend to put it together.

a self-assembly kit = 自行组装套件

self-assertive

② In Britain, people who work for themselves must send in their self-assessment tax return by the end of January.

self-build

self-colour

self-congratulation

self-congratulatory

① The company published a self-congratulatory report about its profits while it was cutting jobs.
= 那家公司一边裁员，一边发布一份对利润自吹自擂的报告。

② His self-congratulatory tone annoyed everyone who had worked hard on the project.

self-conscious
/ˌself ˈkɒnʃəs/
核心：形容词：（在人前）不自在的、难为情的：be self-conscious about something；（文风、手法）刻意的、做作的。副词 self-consciously 是不自在地、刻意地。名词 self-consciousness 另立条。
① She felt self-conscious about her new glasses and kept taking them off in class.
= 她对新戴的眼镜感到难为情，上课时一直摘下来。
be self-conscious about something = 对某事难为情
self-conscious = 难为情的
② The new building has a self-conscious modern style that many people find cold.
= 那栋新楼有一种刻意的现代风格，许多人觉得冷冰冰的。
self-conscious = 刻意的、做作的
③ He smiled self-consciously when everyone at the party started singing "Happy Birthday" to him.
= 聚会上大家开始为他唱《生日快乐》时，他不自在地笑了。
self-consciously = 不自在地

self-consciousness
/ˌself ˈkɒnʃəsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：难为情、局促不安；（文风的）刻意、做作。
① Her self-consciousness disappeared once she started dancing and forgot about the audience.
= 她一开始跳舞、忘了观众，局促不安就消失了。
self-consciousness = 局促不安
② The film suffers from a kind of self-consciousness, as if the director wanted everyone to notice how clever he was.
= 那部电影有一种做作感，仿佛导演想让人人都注意到他有多聪明。
a kind of self-consciousness = 一种做作感

self-contained
/ˌself kənˈteɪnd/
核心：形容词：（公寓、房间）设施齐全独立的：a self-contained flat；（人）独立的、不依赖别人的；（事物）自成一体的、完整的。
① The house has been divided into three self-contained flats, each with its own kitchen and bathroom.
= 那座房子分成了三套独立公寓，每套都有自己的厨房和浴室。
a self-contained flat = 独立公寓
self-contained = 设施齐全独立的
② He is a quiet, self-contained man who does not seem to need other people.
= 他是个沉默独立的人，似乎不需要别人。
a self-contained man = 不依赖别人的人
③ Each chapter of the book is self-contained, so you can read them in any order.
= 那本书的每一章都自成一体，所以可以按任何顺序读。
be self-contained = 自成一体

self-contradiction
/ˌself ˌkɒntrəˈdɪkʃn/
核心：名词，可数或不可数：自相矛盾（的话）。
① His argument is full of self-contradiction; he says taxes are too high and then asks for more spending.
= 他的论点满是自相矛盾：一边说税太高，一边又要求多花钱。
self-contradiction = 自相矛盾
② Telling people that you never lie, while lying to them every day, is a complete self-contradiction.
= 一边每天对人撒谎，一边告诉别人你从不撒谎，这完全是自相矛盾。
a self-contradiction = 自相矛盾的说法

self-contradictory
/ˌself ˌkɒntrəˈdɪktəri/
核心：形容词：自相矛盾的。
① The witness gave a self-contradictory account of the evening, so the police did not believe her.
= 那位证人对当晚的叙述自相矛盾，所以警察不信她。
a self-contradictory account = 自相矛盾的叙述
self-contradictory = 自相矛盾的
② The new rules are self-contradictory: one says you must stay, and another says you must leave.
= 新规定自相矛盾：一条说你必须留下，另一条说你必须离开。
be self-contradictory = 自相矛盾

self-control
/ˌself kənˈtrəʊl/
核心：名词，不可数：自制力、自我控制：lose your self-control、show self-control。
① It took a lot of self-control not to shout at the rude customer in front of everyone.
= 当着大家的面忍住不冲那个无礼的顾客嚷嚷，需要很强的自制力。
self-control = 自制力
② He lost his self-control and hit the man who had insulted his wife.
= 他失去了自制，打了那个侮辱他妻子的人。
lose your self-control = 失去自制

self-controlled
/ˌself kənˈtrəʊld/
核心：形容词：有自制力的、冷静克制的。
① She remained calm and self-controlled throughout the long and difficult trial of her husband.
= 在她丈夫漫长艰难的审判中，她始终保持冷静克制。
self-controlled = 冷静克制的
② The coach wants his players to be self-controlled and not react when the other team tries to annoy them.
= 教练希望队员们冷静克制，对方挑衅时不予理会。
be self-controlled = 有自制力

self-correcting
/ˌself kəˈrektɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我纠正的、自动调节的：a self-correcting system。
① The market is self-correcting, according to some economists, so prices will fall again by themselves.
= 一些经济学家认为，市场会自我纠正，所以价格会自己再降下来。
self-correcting = 自我纠正的
② The new software has a self-correcting function that fixes small errors in the text automatically.
= 那款新软件有自动纠错功能，能自动修正文本里的小错误。
a self-correcting function = 自动纠错功能

self-critical
/ˌself ˈkrɪtɪkl/
核心：形容词：严于律己的、常自我批评的：be self-critical about something。
① She is very self-critical and always thinks her paintings could be better.
= 她非常严于律己，总觉得自己的画还可以更好。
self-critical = 严于律己的
② Good writers are self-critical about their work and rewrite every page many times.
= 好作家对自己的作品很苛刻，每一页都要改写很多遍。
be self-critical about something = 对某事很苛刻

self-criticism
/ˌself ˈkrɪtɪsɪzəm/
核心：名词，不可数：自我批评、自责。
① A little self-criticism is healthy, but too much can make you unhappy.
= 适度的自我批评是健康的，太多则会让你不快乐。
self-criticism = 自我批评
② After the defeat, the team captain spoke with honest self-criticism about his own mistakes.
= 输球之后，队长诚恳地检讨了自己的失误。
honest self-criticism = 诚恳的自我批评

self-defeating
/ˌself dɪˈfiːtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：（做法）弄巧成拙的、适得其反的、自我挫败的。
① Cutting the price too much would be self-defeating, because we would lose money on every sale.
= 降价太多会适得其反，因为每卖一件我们都会亏钱。
self-defeating = 适得其反的
② Shouting at children to make them calm is a self-defeating way to deal with them.
= 冲孩子大喊让他们安静，是一种适得其反的做法。
a self-defeating way = 适得其反的做法

self-defence
/ˌself dɪˈfens/
核心：名词，不可数：自卫；防身术。美式拼作 self-defense。in self-defence 是出于自卫。
① The man said he had hit the burglar in self-defence, and the court agreed.
= 那个男人说他打窃贼是出于自卫，法庭也认可了。
in self-defence = 出于自卫
self-defence = 自卫
② My sister took a course in self-defence before she moved to the big city.
= 我姐姐搬到大城市之前上了一个防身术课程。
a course in self-defence = 防身术课程

self-delusion
/ˌself dɪˈluːʒn/
核心：名词，不可数：自欺、自我欺骗。
① Thinking he could win the election without any money was pure self-delusion.
= 以为不花钱就能赢得选举，纯属自欺欺人。
self-delusion = 自欺
② The novel is about a man whose self-delusion leads him to lose his family and his home.
= 那部小说讲一个男人自欺欺人，最后失去了家人和家园。
somebody's self-delusion = 某人的自欺

self-denial
/ˌself dɪˈnaɪəl/
核心：名词，不可数：自我克制、克己：为别人或某个目标放弃自己想要的东西。
① Her parents lived a life of self-denial so that their children could go to university.
= 她父母过着克己的生活，好让孩子们能上大学。
a life of self-denial = 克己的生活
self-denial = 自我克制
② For a young couple in the city, saving money for a house takes years of self-denial.
= 对城里的年轻夫妇来说，攒钱买房需要多年的自我克制。
years of self-denial = 多年的自我克制

self-deprecating
/ˌself ˈdeprəkeɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自嘲的、谦逊自贬的：self-deprecating humour。
① The famous actor won the audience over with his self-deprecating jokes about his age.
= 那位名演员拿自己的年龄自嘲开玩笑，赢得了观众的好感。
self-deprecating jokes = 自嘲的笑话
self-deprecating = 自嘲的
② She has a charming self-deprecating manner and never boasts about her success.
= 她举止谦逊可爱，从不夸耀自己的成就。
a self-deprecating manner = 谦逊自贬的举止

self-deprecation
/ˌself ˌdeprəˈkeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自嘲、自贬。
① British humour often depends on self-deprecation, which visitors from other countries sometimes find confusing.
= 英式幽默常常靠自嘲，来自其他国家的游客有时会觉得摸不着头脑。
self-deprecation = 自嘲
② There was a note of self-deprecation in his voice when he talked about his first novel.
= 谈到自己的第一部小说时，他的语气里带着几分自嘲。
a note of self-deprecation = 几分自嘲

self-described
/ˌself dɪˈskraɪbd/
核心：形容词，只作定语：自称的、自我描述为……的：a self-described expert。
① My cousin, a self-described food lover, has eaten in every restaurant in the city.
= 我表哥自称是个吃货，城里每家餐馆他都吃过。
a self-described food lover = 自称的吃货
self-described = 自称的
② The self-described expert on the television show made several mistakes about the history of the castle.
= 电视节目上那位自称的专家关于那座城堡的历史说错了好几处。
a self-described expert = 自称的专家

self-destruct
/ˌself dɪˈstrʌkt/
核心：动词，不及物：（机器、武器）自毁、自动销毁；（比喻）（人）自我毁灭。形容词：自毁的：a self-destruct button。
① In the film, the spy's message self-destructs five seconds after he reads it.
= 那部电影里，间谍读完消息五秒后，消息就自动销毁了。
self-destruct = 自动销毁
② The team seemed to self-destruct in the second half, losing four goals in fifteen minutes.
= 那支球队下半场好像自己垮了，十五分钟内丢了四个球。
self-destruct = （比喻）自己垮掉

self-destruction
/ˌself dɪˈstrʌkʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我毁灭、自毁。
① His heavy drinking and fast driving seemed like a slow kind of self-destruction.
= 他酗酒、开快车，看起来像是一种慢性自我毁灭。
self-destruction = 自我毁灭
② The film tells the sad story of a pop star's self-destruction after early fame.
= 那部电影讲述了一位流行歌星少年成名后自我毁灭的悲伤故事。
somebody's self-destruction = 某人的自我毁灭

self-destructive
/ˌself dɪˈstrʌktɪv/
核心：形容词：自我毁灭的、自我伤害的：self-destructive behaviour。
① The doctor was worried about his self-destructive behaviour after his wife left him.
= 妻子离开后，医生很担心他的自我伤害行为。
self-destructive behaviour = 自我伤害的行为
self-destructive = 自我毁灭的
② She had a self-destructive habit of ending every good relationship just when it was going well.
= 她有个自我毁灭的毛病：每段好好的感情一顺利就被她结束掉。
a self-destructive habit = 自我毁灭的习惯

self-determination
/ˌself dɪˌtɜːmɪˈneɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：（民族的）自决、民族自决权；（个人的）自主决定。
① The people of the island voted for self-determination and their own government.
= 那座岛上的人投票支持民族自决，建立自己的政府。
self-determination = 民族自决
② Most teenagers want more self-determination over how they spend their free time at weekends.
= 大多数青少年希望在怎样支配周末空闲时间上有更多自主权。
self-determination over something = 对某事的自主决定

self-development
/ˌself dɪˈveləpmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我发展、自我提升。
① The company encourages staff to spend time on self-development, such as learning a language.
= 那家公司鼓励员工花时间自我提升，比如学一门外语。
self-development = 自我提升
② On the train to work she reads books on self-development and listens to podcasts about confidence.
= 上班的火车上，她读自我提升方面的书，听关于自信的播客。
books on self-development = 自我提升方面的书

self-discovery
/ˌself dɪˈskʌvəri/
核心：名词，不可数：自我发现、认识自我：a journey of self-discovery。
① For many young people, a year of travel is a journey of self-discovery.
= 对许多年轻人来说，一年的旅行是一段认识自我的旅程。
a journey of self-discovery = 认识自我的旅程
self-discovery = 自我发现
② The novel follows a shy girl's slow self-discovery during one summer in Italy.
= 那部小说讲述一个腼腆女孩在意大利的一个夏天里慢慢认识自我的过程。
somebody's self-discovery = 某人的自我发现

self-doubt
/ˌself ˈdaʊt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我怀疑、缺乏自信。
① Even famous writers suffer from self-doubt and wonder whether their next book will be any good.
= 即使是名作家也会自我怀疑，担心下一本书好不好。
suffer from self-doubt = 自我怀疑
self-doubt = 自我怀疑
② After failing the exam, she was full of self-doubt and thought about giving up.
= 考试没通过之后，她满心自我怀疑，想过放弃。
be full of self-doubt = 满心自我怀疑

self-drive
/ˌself ˈdraɪv/
核心：形容词（英式）：（租车）自驾的：a self-drive car、a self-drive holiday。
① We hired a self-drive car at the airport and spent two weeks exploring the island.
= 我们在机场租了一辆自驾车，花两个星期游遍了那座岛。
a self-drive car = 自驾车
self-drive = 自驾的
② The travel company offers self-drive holidays in Scotland with hotels booked in advance.
= 那家旅行社提供苏格兰自驾游，酒店都事先订好。
a self-drive holiday = 自驾游

self-driving
/ˌself ˈdraɪvɪŋ/
核心：形容词：（汽车）自动驾驶的、无人驾驶的：a self-driving car。
① Several companies are testing self-driving cars on the streets of the city.
= 好几家公司正在那座城市的街道上测试自动驾驶汽车。
self-driving cars = 自动驾驶汽车
self-driving = 自动驾驶的
② Many people are still afraid to sit in a self-driving taxi with nobody at the wheel.
= 许多人仍然不敢坐方向盘后面没人的无人驾驶出租车。
a self-driving taxi = 无人驾驶出租车

self-educated
/ˌself ˈedʒukeɪtɪd/
核心：形容词：自学的、自学成才的。
① My grandfather was a self-educated man who left school at twelve but read thousands of books.
= 我祖父是个自学成才的人，十二岁就离开学校，却读了几千本书。
a self-educated man = 自学成才的人
self-educated = 自学的
② The famous scientist was largely self-educated and never went to university or college.
= 那位著名科学家基本上是自学成才的，从没上过大学或学院。
largely self-educated = 基本上自学成才

self-effacement
/ˌself ɪˈfeɪsmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：谦逊低调、不出风头。
① Her self-effacement meant that her colleagues often took the credit for her good ideas.
= 她太低调了，同事们常把她的好主意据为己功。
self-effacement = 谦逊低调
② The old general spoke with typical self-effacement about his years in the war.
= 那位老将军以他一贯的谦逊谈起自己在战争中的岁月。
with self-effacement = 谦逊地

self-effacing
/ˌself ɪˈfeɪsɪŋ/
核心：形容词：谦逊低调的、不爱出风头的。
① He is a shy, self-effacing man who hates talking about his own success.
= 他是个腼腆低调的人，讨厌谈论自己的成就。
self-effacing = 谦逊低调的
② The prize winner gave a short, self-effacing speech and thanked everyone who had helped her.
= 获奖者发表了一篇简短谦逊的讲话，感谢了所有帮助过她的人。
a self-effacing speech = 谦逊的讲话

self-employed
/ˌself ɪmˈplɔɪd/
核心：形容词：个体经营的、自雇的：自己当老板、不受雇于人。也作名词 the self-employed（个体经营者）。名词 self-employment 另立条。
① My father is a self-employed builder who works mostly for families in the village.
= 我父亲是个个体建筑工，主要给村里的人家干活。
self-employed = 个体经营的
② The government has promised new help for the self-employed, who lost a lot of work last year.
= 政府承诺给去年丢了很多活的个体经营者新的帮助。
the self-employed = 个体经营者

self-employment
/ˌself ɪmˈplɔɪmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：个体经营、自雇。
① Self-employment gives you freedom, but you have to find your own customers.
= 个体经营给你自由，但你得自己找客户。
self-employment = 个体经营
② She left the bank for self-employment and now runs a small bakery.
= 她离开银行自己创业，现在开着一家小面包店。
leave a job for self-employment = 辞职自己创业

self-evident
/ˌself ˈevɪdənt/
核心：形容词：不言而喻的、显而易见的。副词 self-evidently 是不言而喻地。
① It is self-evident that children need love and care, but not every child gets them.
= 孩子需要爱和照顾，这不言而喻，可并不是每个孩子都能得到。
self-evident = 不言而喻的
② The plan was self-evidently a bad idea, yet nobody at the meeting said so.
= 那个计划显然是个馊主意，可会上谁也没说。
self-evidently = 显然、不言而喻地

self-examination
/ˌself ɪɡˌzæmɪˈneɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我反省；（医学）自我检查。
① After the defeat, the party went through a long period of self-examination.
= 惨败之后，那个党经历了很长一段时间的自我反省。
self-examination = 自我反省
② Doctors recommend regular self-examination at home to find any unusual lumps as early as possible.
= 医生建议在家定期自我检查，以便尽早发现任何异常肿块。
regular self-examination = 定期自我检查

self-explanatory
/ˌself ɪkˈsplænətri/
核心：形容词：不言自明的、无需解释的。
① The instructions are self-explanatory, so you can put the shelf together without help.
= 说明书一看就懂，所以你不用别人帮忙就能装好架子。
self-explanatory = 不言自明的
② Most of the buttons on the new machine are self-explanatory, but this one needs some explanation.
= 那台新机器上的大多数按钮一看就明白，可这个需要解释一下。
be self-explanatory = 一看就懂

self-expression
/ˌself ɪkˈspreʃn/

① For many teenagers, music, hair and clothes are important forms of self-expression.
= 对许多青少年来说，音乐、发型和衣着是重要的自我表达方式。
a form of self-expression = 自我表达的方式
self-expression = 自我表达
② The art class gives children a chance for self-expression without worrying about mistakes.
= 美术课让孩子们有机会表达自己，而不用担心出错。
a chance for self-expression = 表达自己的机会

self-fulfilling
/ˌself fʊlˈfɪlɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我应验的：多用于 a self-fulfilling prophecy（自我应验的预言：因为相信而成真）。
① If you keep telling yourself you will fail, it can become a self-fulfilling prophecy.
= 你要是一直对自己说会失败，这可能会变成自我应验的预言。
a self-fulfilling prophecy = 自我应验的预言
self-fulfilling = 自我应验的
② Fear of a crisis can be self-fulfilling, as people take their money out of the banks.
= 对危机的恐惧可能会自我应验，因为人们会把钱从银行取出来。
be self-fulfilling = 自我应验

self-fulfilment
/ˌself fʊlˈfɪlmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我实现、实现自我价值。美式拼作 self-fulfillment。
① She found self-fulfilment in teaching rather than in making a lot of money.
= 她在教书中实现了自我价值，而不是在赚大钱中。
find self-fulfilment in something = 在某事中实现自我价值
self-fulfilment = 自我实现
② For him, a job is not just about money; it is also about self-fulfilment.
= 对他来说，工作不只是为了钱，也是为了实现自我。
be about self-fulfilment = 为了实现自我

self-governing
/ˌself ˈɡʌvənɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自治的：a self-governing region。
① The island is a self-governing territory with its own parliament and laws.
= 那座岛是一个自治领地，有自己的议会和法律。
a self-governing territory = 自治领地
self-governing = 自治的
② The school became self-governing and no longer had to follow the local council's rules.
= 那所学校实行了自治，不再需要遵守地方议会的规定。
become self-governing = 实行自治

self-government
/ˌself ˈɡʌvənmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自治、自治政府。
① The mountain region was finally granted self-government after many years of protests.
= 经过多年的抗议，那个山区终于获准自治。
be granted self-government = 获准自治
self-government = 自治
② Self-government means that local people make their own decisions about schools and roads.
= 自治意味着当地人自己决定学校和道路的事。
self-government means = 自治意味着

self-harm
/ˌself ˈhɑːm/
核心：名词，不可数：自残、自我伤害：尤指故意割伤自己。动词：自残。
① The charity runs a helpline for young people who are struggling with self-harm.
= 那家慈善机构为受自残困扰的年轻人开设了一条求助热线。
self-harm = 自残
② Teachers are trained to notice the signs when a student may be self-harming.
= 老师们受过培训，能察觉学生可能在自残的迹象。
self-harm = 自残（动词）

self-help
/ˌself ˈhelp/
核心：名词，不可数：自助、自救：靠自己（而不靠别人）解决问题。作定语：self-help books、a self-help group。
① The shop has a whole section of self-help books about confidence, money and happiness.
= 那家书店有一整个区专卖关于自信、理财和幸福的自助类书籍。
self-help books = 自助类书籍
self-help = 自助
② She joined a self-help group for people who had lost their jobs, and they met every week.
= 她加入了一个失业者互助小组，他们每周聚一次。
a self-help group = 互助小组

self-identification
/ˌself aɪˌdentɪfɪˈkeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我认同：把自己看作属于某一群体或某种身份。
① Language is an important part of self-identification for many minority groups in the country.
= 对这个国家的许多少数群体来说，语言是自我认同的重要部分。
self-identification = 自我认同
② The survey relies on self-identification, so people choose the group they feel they belong to.
= 那项调查依据自我认同，所以人们自己选择觉得所属的群体。
rely on self-identification = 依据自我认同

self-identify
/ˌself aɪˈdentɪfaɪ/
核心：动词，不及物：自我认定为、认同自己是：self-identify as something。
① About a third of the students self-identify as religious, according to the university survey.
= 据大学的调查，大约三分之一的学生认为自己信教。
self-identify as something = 自我认定为某种身份
self-identify = 自我认定
② In the new census, people can self-identify as belonging to more than one group.
= 在新的人口普查里，人们可以自我认定属于不止一个群体。
self-identify as belonging to = 自认属于

self-identity
/ˌself aɪˈdentəti/
核心：名词，不可数：自我身份、自我认同感。
① Losing his job as a pilot shook his self-identity, because flying had been his whole life.
= 丢了飞行员的工作动摇了他的自我认同，因为飞行曾是他的全部生活。
self-identity = 自我认同感
② Teenagers often try out different styles of music and clothes as they form their self-identity.
= 青少年在形成自我身份的过程中，常常尝试不同风格的音乐和衣着。
form your self-identity = 形成自我身份

self-image
/ˌself ˈɪmɪdʒ/
核心：名词，可数或不可数：自我形象：一个人对自己的看法。
① Advertisements full of perfect bodies can seriously damage young people's self-image and confidence.
= 满是完美身材的广告可能严重损害年轻人的自我形象和自信。
self-image = 自我形象
② Winning the prize gave her a much more positive self-image, and she started to sing in public.
= 获奖让她对自己有了更积极的看法，她开始当众唱歌。
a positive self-image = 积极的自我形象

self-immolation
/ˌself ˌɪməˈleɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自焚（以示抗议）；（正式）自我牺牲。
① The monk's self-immolation in the square was a protest against the war.
= 那位僧人在广场上自焚，是为了抗议那场战争。
self-immolation = 自焚
② The newspapers reported two cases of self-immolation during the protests last year.
= 报纸报道了去年抗议期间发生的两起自焚事件。
cases of self-immolation = 自焚事件

self-importance
/ˌself ɪmˈpɔːtns/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：妄自尊大、自以为了不起。
① His self-importance annoyed everyone in the office, especially when he called himself "the boss".
= 他妄自尊大，惹恼了办公室里的每个人，尤其是他自称「老板」的时候。
self-importance = 妄自尊大
② The comedian makes fun of the self-importance of politicians who think they know everything.
= 那位喜剧演员嘲讽那些自以为无所不知、妄自尊大的政客。
the self-importance of somebody = 某人的妄自尊大

self-important
/ˌself ɪmˈpɔːtnt/
核心：形容词（贬义）：妄自尊大的、自以为了不起的。副词 self-importantly 是自以为了不起地。
① A self-important little man at the desk told us we would have to wait for an hour.
= 前台一个自以为了不起的小个子男人告诉我们得等一个小时。
self-important = 妄自尊大的
② The young manager walked self-importantly around the office, giving orders to everyone.
= 那个年轻经理自以为了不起地在办公室里走来走去，对每个人发号施令。
self-importantly = 自以为了不起地

self-imposed
/ˌself ɪmˈpəʊzd/
核心：形容词：自己强加的、自愿承担的：a self-imposed deadline、self-imposed exile。
① The writer set himself a self-imposed deadline of one chapter a week.
= 那位作家给自己定了个期限：每周写一章。
a self-imposed deadline = 自定的期限
self-imposed = 自己强加的
② After the scandal, the singer went into self-imposed exile in a small village in France.
= 丑闻过后，那位歌手自我放逐到法国的一个小村子里。
self-imposed exile = 自我放逐

self-improvement
/ˌself ɪmˈpruːvmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我完善、自我提高。
① He spends his evenings on self-improvement, learning Spanish and reading history books.
= 他晚上都用来自我提高，学西班牙语、读历史书。
self-improvement = 自我提高
② The bookshop has a large section of books on self-improvement and healthy living.
= 那家书店有一大块区域专卖自我提高和健康生活方面的书。
books on self-improvement = 自我提高方面的书

self-induced
/ˌself ɪnˈdjuːst/
核心：形容词：自己造成的、自找的：self-induced problems。
① Many of his health problems were self-induced, caused by years of smoking and drinking.
= 他的许多健康问题是自己造成的，是多年抽烟喝酒的结果。
self-induced = 自己造成的
② The team's defeat was self-induced, because they gave away two easy goals.
= 那支球队的失败是自找的，因为他们送了对方两个轻松的进球。
a self-induced defeat = 自找的失败

self-indulgence
/ˌself ɪnˈdʌldʒəns/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自我放纵、放任自己。
① Buying a new car every year is pure self-indulgence when you only drive to the shops.
= 只是开车去商店，却每年都买新车，纯属放纵自己。
self-indulgence = 自我放纵
② A box of chocolates on a Friday evening is my one small self-indulgence.
= 星期五晚上吃一盒巧克力，是我唯一的一点小放纵。
a small self-indulgence = 小小的放纵

self-indulgent
/ˌself ɪnˈdʌldʒənt/
核心：形容词（贬义）：放纵自己的、只顾自己享受的；（作品）任性的、自我陶醉的。
① It felt a bit self-indulgent to stay in bed until noon, but I was very tired.
= 一直赖床到中午感觉有点放纵自己，可我真的很累。
self-indulgent = 放纵自己的
② Critics called the film long and self-indulgent, full of scenes that the director loved but nobody else did.
= 评论家说那部电影冗长而自我陶醉，满是导演自己喜欢、别人都不喜欢的镜头。
a self-indulgent film = 自我陶醉的电影

self-inflicted
/ˌself ɪnˈflɪktɪd/
核心：形容词：（伤害、问题）自己造成的、自讨的：a self-inflicted wound。
① The doctors said the wound was self-inflicted, and the soldier admitted that he had shot himself.
= 医生说那伤是他自己造成的，那名士兵也承认是自己开的枪。
a self-inflicted wound = 自伤
self-inflicted = 自己造成的
② The company's problems are largely self-inflicted, caused by bad decisions at the top.
= 那家公司的问题主要是自找的，是高层决策失误造成的。
largely self-inflicted = 主要是自找的

self-interest
/ˌself ˈɪntrəst/
核心：名词，不可数：私利、自身利益。
① He only helped us out of self-interest, because he wanted us to vote for him.
= 他帮我们只是出于私利，因为他想让我们投他的票。
out of self-interest = 出于私利
self-interest = 私利
② It is in our own self-interest to protect the river, because the town depends on it.
= 保护那条河符合我们自身的利益，因为全镇都靠它。
in your own self-interest = 符合自身利益

self-interested
/ˌself ˈɪntrəstɪd/
核心：形容词（常含贬义）：自私自利的、只顾私利的。
① The decision was self-interested, made to protect the managers rather than the workers.
= 那个决定是自私自利的，是为了保护经理而不是工人。
self-interested = 自私自利的
② Voters are tired of self-interested politicians who forget their promises after the election.
= 选民厌倦了那些只顾私利、选后就忘了承诺的政客。
self-interested politicians = 只顾私利的政客

self-involved
/ˌself ɪnˈvɒlvd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只关心自己的、自我陶醉的。
① He was too self-involved to notice that his friend was having a hard time.
= 他太只顾自己了，没注意到朋友正过得很艰难。
self-involved = 只关心自己的
② The novel's main character is a self-involved young man who learns to care about others.
= 那部小说的主人公是一个只顾自己的年轻人，后来学会了关心别人。
a self-involved young man = 只顾自己的年轻人

self-knowledge
/ˌself ˈnɒlɪdʒ/
核心：名词，不可数：自知之明、对自己的了解。
① Self-knowledge comes with age, as we learn what makes us happy and what makes us angry.
= 自知之明随年龄而来，我们会慢慢明白什么让自己快乐、什么让自己生气。
self-knowledge = 自知之明
② The course helps managers gain self-knowledge about how they deal with stress.
= 那门课程帮助管理者更了解自己如何应对压力。
gain self-knowledge = 加深对自己的了解

self-loathing
/ˌself ˈləʊðɪŋ/
核心：名词，不可数：自我厌恶、自我憎恨。
① After he hurt his friend, he was filled with self-loathing for weeks.
= 伤害了朋友之后，他好几个星期都充满了自我厌恶。
be filled with self-loathing = 充满自我厌恶
self-loathing = 自我厌恶
② The singer wrote about the self-loathing she felt as a teenager in her new song.
= 那位歌手在新歌里写了她少女时代感受到的自我厌恶。
feel self-loathing = 感到自我厌恶

self-love
/ˌself ˈlʌv/
核心：名词，不可数：自爱；（贬义）自恋、自私。
① The book teaches that a little self-love is necessary before you can love other people.
= 那本书讲，你要先有一点自爱，才能去爱别人。
self-love = 自爱
② His self-love was so great that he could not accept any criticism at all.
= 他自恋到了极点，一点批评都接受不了。
self-love = 自恋

self-made
/ˌself ˈmeɪd/
核心：形容词：白手起家的、靠自己奋斗成功的：a self-made man / millionaire。
① My grandfather was a self-made man who started with nothing and built a big company.
= 我祖父是个白手起家的人，从一无所有做到创办了一家大公司。
a self-made man = 白手起家的人
self-made = 白手起家的
② She is a self-made millionaire who left school at sixteen and opened her first shop at twenty.
= 她是白手起家的百万富翁，十六岁辍学，二十岁开了第一家店。
a self-made millionaire = 白手起家的百万富翁

self-management
/ˌself ˈmænɪdʒmənt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我管理；（疾病的）自我照护。
① Self-management is an important skill for people who work from home without a boss.
= 对没有老板、在家工作的人来说，自我管理是一项重要技能。
self-management = 自我管理
② At the clinic, the nurse teaches patients with diabetes the self-management of their condition.
= 在诊所里，那位护士教糖尿病人自我照护自己的病情。
the self-management of a condition = 病情的自我照护

self-medicate
/ˌself ˈmedɪkeɪt/
核心：动词，不及物：自行用药：不经医生自己吃药；（贬义）借酒、药物逃避问题。
① Many people self-medicate with painkillers instead of going to see a doctor.
= 许多人不去看医生，而是自己吃止痛药。
self-medicate = 自行用药
② After his divorce, he started to self-medicate with alcohol to forget his problems.
= 离婚后，他开始借酒浇愁，忘掉烦恼。
self-medicate with alcohol = 借酒逃避

self-motivated
/ˌself ˈməʊtɪveɪtɪd/
核心：形容词：自我激励的、积极主动的：不需要别人督促。
① We are looking for a self-motivated person who can work alone and solve problems.
= 我们在找一个积极主动、能独立工作和解决问题的人。
a self-motivated person = 积极主动的人
self-motivated = 自我激励的
② Students who study online need to be self-motivated, because nobody checks their work every day.
= 上网课的学生需要积极主动，因为没人每天检查他们的作业。
be self-motivated = 积极主动

self-motivation
/ˌself ˌməʊtɪˈveɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我激励、自觉性。
① Running a marathon takes months of training and a lot of self-motivation.
= 跑马拉松需要几个月的训练和很强的自我激励。
self-motivation = 自我激励
② Her self-motivation impressed her teachers, who never had to tell her to do her homework.
= 她的自觉性给老师们留下了深刻印象，他们从来不用叫她做作业。
somebody's self-motivation = 某人的自觉性

self-mutilation
/ˌself ˌmjuːtɪˈleɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自残、自伤肢体。
① The young prisoner was moved to a hospital after several acts of self-mutilation in his cell.
= 那名年轻囚犯在牢房里几次自残后，被转到了医院。
acts of self-mutilation = 自残行为
self-mutilation = 自残
② Doctors say self-mutilation is often a sign of deep pain that a person cannot talk about.
= 医生说，自残往往是一个人说不出口的深层痛苦的信号。
self-mutilation is a sign = 自残是一种信号

self-obsessed
/ˌself əbˈsest/
核心：形容词（贬义）：极度自我关注的、只关心自己的。
① The film is about a self-obsessed actor who cannot stop looking at himself in the mirror.
= 那部电影讲一个极度自恋、一直照镜子停不下来的演员。
self-obsessed = 极度自我关注的
② Some people say that social media is making young people more self-obsessed.
= 有人说社交媒体正让年轻人越来越只关心自己。
more self-obsessed = 越来越只关心自己

self-opinionated
/ˌself əˈpɪnjəneɪtɪd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自以为是的、固执己见的。
① He is a loud, self-opinionated man who never listens to anyone else's ideas.
= 他嗓门大、自以为是，从来不听别人的意见。
self-opinionated = 自以为是的
② The self-opinionated critic wrote that nobody else understood the film except him.
= 那个自以为是的评论家写道，除了他谁都看不懂那部电影。
a self-opinionated critic = 自以为是的评论家

self-perpetuating
/ˌself pəˈpetʃueɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我延续的、恶性循环的：一旦开始就会自己持续下去。
① Poverty can be self-perpetuating, because poor children often have fewer chances at school.
= 贫穷可能会自我延续，因为穷孩子在学校的机会往往更少。
self-perpetuating = 自我延续的
② Fear of crime is self-perpetuating: people stay at home, so the streets become emptier and less safe.
= 对犯罪的恐惧会形成恶性循环：人们待在家里，街上就更空、更不安全。
be self-perpetuating = 形成恶性循环

self-pity
/ˌself ˈpɪti/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自怜、自哀自怨。
① Instead of drowning in self-pity after losing his job, he started his own small business.
= 他失业后没有沉溺于自怜，而是自己开了一家小公司。
drown in self-pity = 沉溺于自怜
self-pity = 自怜
常带批评口气，说人沉溺于自己的不幸、不肯振作。
② There was a note of self-pity in her voice when she talked about her unhappy childhood.
= 她谈到不幸的童年时，声音里带着几分自怜。
a note of self-pity = 几分自怜

self-pitying
/ˌself ˈpɪtiɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自怜的、自哀自怨的。
① He wrote a long self-pitying letter about how badly everyone had treated him.
= 他写了一封长长的自怜信，说大家对他有多坏。
a self-pitying letter = 自怜的信
self-pitying = 自怜的
② Stop being so self-pitying all the time and do something to change the situation.
= 别总那么自哀自怨了，做点什么改变现状吧。
be self-pitying = 自哀自怨

self-portrait
/ˌself ˈpɔːtreɪt/
核心：名词，可数：自画像；（比喻）自我描写。
① The museum has a self-portrait that the artist painted when he was twenty and very poor.
= 那座博物馆有一幅那位画家二十岁、穷困潦倒时画的自画像。
a self-portrait = 自画像
② Her first novel is really a self-portrait of a lonely girl growing up in the 1970s.
= 她的第一部小说其实是一幅自我写照，写的是二十世纪七十年代一个孤独女孩的成长。
a self-portrait of somebody = 某人的自我写照

self-possessed
/ˌself pəˈzest/
核心：形容词：沉着冷静的、镇定自若的。
① For a girl of fifteen, she was very self-possessed and answered the reporters' questions calmly.
= 作为一个十五岁的女孩，她非常镇定，从容地回答了记者的提问。
self-possessed = 沉着冷静的
② He remained self-possessed even when the angry crowd started shouting at him.
= 即使愤怒的人群开始冲他大喊，他依然镇定自若。
remain self-possessed = 保持镇定

self-possession
/ˌself pəˈzeʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：沉着、镇定。
① She showed great self-possession when the fire alarm went off in the middle of her speech.
= 她演讲到一半火警响了，她表现得非常镇定。
show self-possession = 表现得镇定
self-possession = 沉着
② He lost his self-possession for a moment, then smiled and continued as if nothing had happened.
= 他一时失了镇定，随即笑了笑，像什么都没发生一样继续下去。
lose your self-possession = 失去镇定

self-preservation
/ˌself ˌprezəˈveɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我保护、自保：the instinct of self-preservation。
① A strong instinct of self-preservation made him jump out of the way of the car.
= 强烈的自保本能让他一下子跳开，躲过了那辆车。
the instinct of self-preservation = 自保本能
self-preservation = 自我保护
② The minister lied to parliament out of self-preservation, hoping to keep his job.
= 那位部长出于自保对议会撒了谎，希望保住职位。
out of self-preservation = 出于自保

self-proclaimed
/ˌself prəˈkleɪmd/
核心：形容词，只作定语（常含贬义）：自封的、自称的：a self-proclaimed leader。
① The self-proclaimed king of the island had no real power at all.
= 那个自封为岛上国王的人根本没有任何实权。
self-proclaimed = 自封的
② My brother, a self-proclaimed expert on football, tells everyone who will win every match.
= 我哥哥自封为足球专家，逢人就说每场比赛谁会赢。
a self-proclaimed expert = 自封的专家

self-promotion
/ˌself prəˈməʊʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自我宣传、自我推销。
① The singer's constant self-promotion on social media annoyed some of her fans.
= 那位歌手在社交媒体上没完没了的自我宣传惹恼了一些歌迷。
self-promotion = 自我宣传
② In some jobs, a little self-promotion is necessary if you want to be noticed.
= 在有些工作里，想被人注意到，就得有点自我推销。
a little self-promotion = 一点自我推销

self-realization
/ˌself ˌriːəlaɪˈzeɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我实现、实现自我潜能。英式也拼作 self-realisation。
① For the philosopher, education was a path to self-realization, not just a way to get a job.
= 对那位哲学家来说，教育是通往自我实现的道路，而不只是找工作的手段。
self-realization = 自我实现
② Many people find self-realization in art, music, gardening or caring for others.
= 许多人在艺术、音乐、园艺或照顾他人中实现自我。
find self-realization = 实现自我

self-referential
/ˌself ˌrefəˈrenʃl/
核心：形容词：自我指涉的、指向自身的：（作品）谈论或模仿自身的。
① The television comedy is very self-referential, with jokes about its own actors and earlier episodes.
= 那部电视喜剧很爱自我指涉，常拿自己的演员和前几集开玩笑。
self-referential = 自我指涉的
② Some modern novels are self-referential, with characters who know they are in a book.
= 有些现代小说会指涉自身，里面的人物知道自己在一本书里。
be self-referential = 指涉自身

self-reflection
/ˌself rɪˈflekʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我反省、反躬自省。
① The long walk in the mountains gave her time for self-reflection after a difficult year.
= 经历了艰难的一年，在山里长途徒步让她有时间反省自己。
time for self-reflection = 自我反省的时间
self-reflection = 自我反省
② Writing a diary every night encourages self-reflection and helps you understand your feelings.
= 每晚写日记能促进自我反省，帮你理解自己的情绪。
encourage self-reflection = 促进自我反省

self-regard
/ˌself rɪˈɡɑːd/
核心：名词，不可数：自尊；（贬义）自负、过分看重自己。
① Doing well at school helped to build the boy's self-regard after years of failure.
= 多年失败之后，在学校表现好帮那个男孩建立了自尊。
self-regard = 自尊
② The actor's enormous self-regard made him very difficult to work with on the film.
= 那个演员极为自负，拍片时很难与之共事。
enormous self-regard = 极度自负

self-regarding
/ˌself rɪˈɡɑːdɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只顾自己的、自私的。
① The critic called the book self-regarding, because the writer only talked about his own feelings.
= 那位评论家说那本书只顾自我，因为作者只谈自己的感受。
self-regarding = 只顾自己的
② She was tired of his self-regarding stories about how clever he was at work.
= 她厌倦了他那些讲自己工作上有多聪明的自吹故事。
self-regarding stories = 自吹自擂的故事

self-regulating
/ˌself ˈreɡjuleɪtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我调节的、自律的：a self-regulating industry。
① The body is a self-regulating system that keeps its temperature at about thirty-seven degrees.
= 人体是一个自我调节的系统，能把体温保持在三十七度左右。
a self-regulating system = 自我调节的系统
self-regulating = 自我调节的
② The newspapers want to remain self-regulating rather than be controlled by the government.
= 报业希望保持自律，而不是受政府管控。
remain self-regulating = 保持自律

self-regulation
/ˌself ˌreɡjuˈleɪʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：（行业的）自律、自我监管；（个人的）自我调节。
① Critics say that self-regulation of the banks failed to stop the financial crisis.
= 批评者说，银行业的自律没能阻止那场金融危机。
self-regulation = 自律
② Young children are still learning self-regulation and often cannot control their anger.
= 幼儿还在学习自我调节，常常控制不住怒气。
learn self-regulation = 学习自我调节

self-reliance
/ˌself rɪˈlaɪəns/
核心：名词，不可数：自立、自力更生。
① Living alone in the countryside for a year taught her self-reliance and practical skills.
= 独自在乡下住了一年，教会了她自立和实用的本领。
self-reliance = 自立
多用于个人，也可说国家经济的自力更生。
② The school encourages self-reliance by letting older children organize their own trips.
= 那所学校让高年级学生自己组织出游，鼓励他们自立。
encourage self-reliance = 鼓励自立

self-report
/ˌself rɪˈpɔːt/
核心：名词，可数：（研究中的）自我报告、自述。动词：自我报告。多作定语：self-report data。
① The study is based on self-report, so people may not always tell the truth about how much they drink.
= 那项研究基于自我报告，所以人们未必总会如实说自己喝多少酒。
self-report = 自我报告
② Patients self-report their pain on a scale from one to ten every morning.
= 病人每天早上按一到十的等级自己报告疼痛程度。
self-report = 自我报告（动词）

self-respect
/ˌself rɪˈspekt/
核心：名词，不可数：自尊、自重。
① Losing his job took away his self-respect, and he stopped looking after himself.
= 失业夺走了他的自尊，他也不再照顾自己了。
self-respect = 自尊
偏「自重、有骨气」，指不肯做降低身份、有失尊严的事。
② No one with any self-respect would accept such a rude offer from the company.
= 有点自尊心的人都不会接受那家公司那么无礼的条件。
anyone with self-respect = 有自尊心的人

self-respecting
/ˌself rɪˈspektɪŋ/
核心：形容词，只作定语：自重的、有自尊的；（幽默）够格的、像样的：no self-respecting cat would…。
① No self-respecting chef would serve frozen vegetables in an expensive restaurant like this.
= 稍有自尊的厨师都不会在这样的高档餐馆里上冷冻蔬菜。
no self-respecting chef = 稍有自尊的厨师
self-respecting = 自重的
② Every self-respecting teenager wants the latest phone, according to the advertisements on television.
= 按电视广告的说法，每个像样的青少年都想要最新款的手机。
every self-respecting teenager = 每个像样的青少年

self-restraint
/ˌself rɪˈstreɪnt/
核心：名词，不可数：自我克制、自制。
① It took a lot of self-restraint not to eat the whole box of chocolates at once.
= 忍住不一口气吃光一整盒巧克力，需要很强的自制力。
self-restraint = 自我克制
② The police showed great self-restraint when the protesters threw bottles at them.
= 抗议者朝警察扔瓶子时，警察表现得非常克制。
show self-restraint = 表现得克制

self-righteous
/ˌself ˈraɪtʃəs/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自以为是的、自命清高的：觉得只有自己对、道德比别人高。副词 self-righteously 是自以为是地。名词 self-righteousness 另立条。
① I hate his self-righteous tone when he tells everyone else how they should live.
= 他告诉别人该怎么生活时那种自以为是的口气，我很讨厌。
a self-righteous tone = 自以为是的口气
self-righteous = 自以为是的
② "I would never do such a thing," she said self-righteously, although we all knew she had.
= 「我绝不会做那种事。」她自命清高地说，可我们都知道她做过。
self-righteously = 自以为是地

self-righteousness
/ˌself ˈraɪtʃəsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数（贬义）：自以为是、自命清高。
① The self-righteousness of the new manager made him unpopular with the older staff.
= 新经理的自以为是让老员工很不待见他。
self-righteousness = 自以为是
② The film makes fun of the self-righteousness of people who judge others but ignore their own faults.
= 那部电影嘲讽了那些评判别人、却无视自己毛病的人的自命清高。
the self-righteousness of somebody = 某人的自命清高

self-rule
/ˌself ˈruːl/
核心：名词，不可数：自治：一个国家或地区自己管理自己。
① After a hundred years of foreign control, the country finally won self-rule in 1960.
= 经过一百年的外国统治，那个国家终于在一九六〇年赢得了自治。
win self-rule = 赢得自治
self-rule = 自治
② The islanders voted for self-rule, but they wanted to keep close ties with the mainland.
= 岛民们投票赞成自治，但希望和大陆保持密切联系。
vote for self-rule = 投票赞成自治

self-sacrifice
/ˌself ˈsækrɪfaɪs/
核心：名词，不可数：自我牺牲：为了别人放弃自己的利益。
① Her parents' self-sacrifice made it possible for her to study medicine at a good university.
= 她父母的自我牺牲让她得以在一所好大学学医。
self-sacrifice = 自我牺牲
② The film celebrates the self-sacrifice of the nurses who worked through the war.
= 那部电影颂扬了在战争中坚持工作的护士们的自我牺牲。
the self-sacrifice of somebody = 某人的自我牺牲

self-sacrificing
/ˌself ˈsækrɪfaɪsɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我牺牲的、舍己为人的。
① She was a kind, self-sacrificing woman who always put her family before herself.
= 她是个善良、舍己为人的女人，总是把家人放在自己前面。
self-sacrificing = 舍己为人的
② The self-sacrificing doctor gave up her holidays to work in the poorest parts of the city.
= 那位舍己为人的医生放弃了假期，到城里最穷的地方工作。
a self-sacrificing doctor = 舍己为人的医生

self-satisfaction
/ˌself ˌsætɪsˈfækʃn/
核心：名词，不可数（常含贬义）：自满、沾沾自喜。
① He smiled with obvious self-satisfaction when the boss praised his report in front of everyone.
= 老板当众表扬他的报告时，他明显沾沾自喜地笑了。
with self-satisfaction = 沾沾自喜地
self-satisfaction = 自满
② There was no room for self-satisfaction, because the team had only won by a single point.
= 没有自满的余地，因为球队只赢了一分。
no room for self-satisfaction = 没有自满的余地

self-satisfied
/ˌself ˈsætɪsfaɪd/
核心：形容词（贬义）：自满的、沾沾自喜的：a self-satisfied smile。
① He had a self-satisfied smile on his face after he won the argument.
= 他吵赢之后，脸上带着沾沾自喜的笑容。
a self-satisfied smile = 沾沾自喜的笑容
self-satisfied = 自满的
② The government report strongly criticized the self-satisfied attitude of the company's managers.
= 那份政府报告严厉批评了公司管理层自满的态度。
a self-satisfied attitude = 自满的态度

self-seeking
/ˌself ˈsiːkɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：追逐私利的、自私的。名词，不可数：追逐私利。
① The voters were tired of self-seeking politicians who cared only about their own careers.
= 选民厌倦了那些只关心自己仕途、追逐私利的政客。
self-seeking politicians = 追逐私利的政客
self-seeking = 追逐私利的
② The charity was damaged by the self-seeking of a few of its leaders.
= 那家慈善机构因少数领导人追逐私利而受损。
the self-seeking of somebody = 某人的追逐私利

self-selected
/ˌself sɪˈlektɪd/
核心：形容词：（样本、人群）自选的、自愿加入的（而非随机抽取）。
① The survey used a self-selected group of people who chose to answer the questions online.
= 那项调查用的是一组自愿在网上答题的人。
a self-selected group = 自选的群体
self-selected = 自选的
② Results from a self-selected sample may not show what the whole population thinks.
= 自选样本得出的结果未必代表全体人口的想法。
a self-selected sample = 自选样本

self-selecting
/ˌself sɪˈlektɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自行选择的、自愿参与的：self-selecting groups。
① The class for advanced students is self-selecting, because only the keenest choose to join it.
= 那个高级班是自愿参加的，因为只有最积极的学生才会选。
self-selecting = 自愿参与的
② Online polls are self-selecting, so they often attract people with very strong opinions.
= 网上投票是自愿参加的，所以常常吸引观点非常强烈的人。
self-selecting polls = 自愿参与的投票

self-selection
/ˌself sɪˈlekʃn/
核心：名词，不可数：自我选择：（研究中）参与者自行决定是否参加，可能造成偏差。
① Self-selection is a problem in the study, because only healthy people chose to take part.
= 自我选择是那项研究的一个问题，因为只有健康的人选择参加。
self-selection = 自我选择
② The researchers tried to avoid self-selection by choosing people at random from the phone book.
= 研究人员从电话簿里随机挑选参与者，设法避免自我选择偏差。
avoid self-selection = 避免自我选择偏差

self-service
/ˌself ˈsɜːvɪs/
核心：形容词：自助式的：a self-service restaurant、self-service checkouts。名词，不可数：自助（服务）。
① We had lunch in a self-service restaurant where you carry your own tray to the table.
= 我们在一家自助餐厅吃了午饭，那里要自己端着托盘到桌边。
a self-service restaurant = 自助餐厅
self-service = 自助式的
② Most petrol stations now are self-service, so you fill the car yourself and pay inside.
= 现在大多数加油站都是自助的，你自己加油，然后进去付钱。
be self-service = 是自助的

self-serving
/ˌself ˈsɜːvɪŋ/
核心：形容词（贬义）：只为自己谋利的、自私自利的。
① The minister's self-serving explanation for the mistake did not convince anyone in parliament.
= 那位部长对失误的辩解只为自己开脱，没能说服议会里的任何人。
a self-serving explanation = 只为自己开脱的辩解
self-serving = 自私自利的
② Many voters see the new law as self-serving, because it protects the politicians who wrote it.
= 许多选民认为那项新法律是为自己谋利，因为它保护的正是起草它的政客。
be self-serving = 只为自己谋利

self-starter
/ˌself ˈstɑːtə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：积极主动的人（不需督促就能做事），常见于招聘广告；（旧）（汽车的）自动启动器。
① The company is looking for a self-starter who can work without much supervision.
= 那家公司在找一个不太需要监督、积极主动的人。
a self-starter = 积极主动的人
② She is a real self-starter who set up her own business at the age of nineteen.
= 她真是个积极主动的人，十九岁就自己创业了。
a real self-starter = 真正积极主动的人

self-storage
/ˌself ˈstɔːrɪdʒ/
核心：名词，不可数：自助仓储：租用小仓库存放个人物品。
① We put our furniture into self-storage while we were living abroad for a year.
= 我们在国外住一年期间，把家具放进了自助仓库。
self-storage = 自助仓储
② A new self-storage building has opened on the edge of town with hundreds of small units.
= 镇边新开了一座自助仓储楼，有几百个小隔间。
a self-storage building = 自助仓储楼

self-study
/ˌself ˈstʌdi/
核心：名词，不可数：自学：不靠老师自己学习。作定语：self-study materials。
① My grandfather learned three languages through self-study, using old books and the radio.
= 我祖父靠旧书和收音机自学了三门语言。
through self-study = 靠自学
self-study = 自学
② The course comes with self-study materials so that students can practise at home.
= 那门课程附有自学材料，让学生可以在家练习。
self-study materials = 自学材料

self-styled
/ˌself ˈstaɪld/
核心：形容词，只作定语（常含贬义）：自封的、自称的：a self-styled expert。
① A self-styled expert on the radio said that the earthquake had been caused by the moon.
= 电台里一个自封的专家说那场地震是月亮引起的。
a self-styled expert = 自封的专家
self-styled = 自封的
② The self-styled king of rock and roll had only ever sold a few hundred records.
= 那个自称摇滚之王的人一共只卖出过几百张唱片。
the self-styled king = 自称的「之王」

self-sufficiency
/ˌself səˈfɪʃnsi/
核心：名词，不可数：自给自足、自立：不依赖外部供给。
① The farm aims at complete self-sufficiency, growing all its own food and making its own electricity.
= 那座农场以完全自给自足为目标，粮食全靠自己种，电也自己发。
complete self-sufficiency = 完全自给自足
self-sufficiency = 自给自足
② The government wants the country to reach self-sufficiency in energy within twenty years.
= 政府希望国家在二十年内实现能源自给。
self-sufficiency in something = 某方面的自给自足
用于国家时多指粮食、能源不依赖进口。

self-sufficient
/ˌself səˈfɪʃnt/
核心：形容词：自给自足的：be self-sufficient in something；（人）自立的、不依赖别人的。
① The island is self-sufficient in vegetables, but it has to bring in meat and fuel by boat.
= 那座岛蔬菜能自给自足，但肉和燃料得靠船运进来。
be self-sufficient in something = 某方面自给自足
self-sufficient = 自给自足的
② Living alone in the mountains made her very self-sufficient and good at fixing things.
= 独自住在山里让她变得非常自立，也很会修东西。
very self-sufficient = 非常自立
说人时有时略带「不需要别人、难以亲近」的意味。

self-supporting
/ˌself səˈpɔːtɪŋ/
核心：形容词：（人、机构）自立的、自负盈亏的：不靠别人资助；（建筑结构）自承重的。
① The small theatre is now self-supporting and no longer needs money from the council.
= 那家小剧院如今自负盈亏，不再需要市政拨款了。
self-supporting = 自负盈亏的
② The tent has a self-supporting frame, so you do not need to tie it to the ground.
= 那顶帐篷有自承重的支架，所以不用把它系在地上。
a self-supporting frame = 自承重的支架
说人时指经济上自立，比如学生边工边读、不靠父母。

self-sustaining
/ˌself səˈsteɪnɪŋ/
核心：形容词：自我维持的、能自行持续的：a self-sustaining economy、self-sustaining growth。
① The project will be self-sustaining once the village shop starts making a profit.
= 一旦村里的商店开始盈利，那个项目就能自我维持了。
self-sustaining = 自我维持的
② Experts hope the new industry will lead to self-sustaining growth in the region.
= 专家希望新产业能带动那个地区实现可持续的自主增长。
self-sustaining growth = 可持续的自主增长
多用于经济、生态、项目，强调不再需要外部投入。

self-taught
/ˌself ˈtɔːt/
核心：形容词：自学的、自学成才的：a self-taught artist。
① He is a self-taught artist who never went to art school but sells his paintings all over the world.
= 他是个自学成才的画家，从没上过美术学校，画却卖到了世界各地。
a self-taught artist = 自学成才的画家
self-taught = 自学的
② My mother is a self-taught cook who learned everything from her grandmother.
= 我妈妈做饭是自学的，全是跟她祖母学的。
a self-taught cook = 自学的厨师
常带赞许，强调没有正式训练也有真本事。

self-timer
/ˌself ˈtaɪmə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：（相机的）自拍定时器、自动定时拍摄装置。
① We put the camera on a wall and used the self-timer so that the whole family could be in the photo.
= 我们把相机放在墙上，用自拍定时器，好让全家人都拍进照片里。
the self-timer = 自拍定时器
② Set the self-timer for ten seconds and then run and stand next to the others.
= 把自拍定时设成十秒，然后跑过去站到别人旁边。
set the self-timer = 设定自拍定时
手机相机也有这个功能，常在设置里叫「定时」。

self-titled
/ˌself ˈtaɪtld/
核心：形容词：（专辑、作品）以作者或乐队名字命名的、同名的。
① The band's first album was self-titled, simply using the name of the group.
= 那支乐队的首张专辑是同名专辑，直接用了乐队的名字。
self-titled = 同名的
② The young singer's self-titled debut sold a million copies in its first year.
= 那位年轻歌手的同名首张专辑第一年就卖了一百万张。
a self-titled debut = 同名首作
多用于音乐专辑，也用于以主角名命名的电视节目。

self-willed
/ˌself ˈwɪld/
核心：形容词（常含贬义）：任性的、固执己见的。
① She was a self-willed child who always did exactly what she wanted, whatever her parents said.
= 她是个任性的孩子，不管父母说什么，总是想干什么就干什么。
a self-willed child = 任性的孩子
self-willed = 任性的
② The self-willed old man refused to move out of his house, even when it began to fall down.
= 那个固执的老人哪怕房子开始塌了也不肯搬出去。
self-willed = 固执己见的
比 stubborn 更偏「凡事只按自己意愿」。

self-worth
/ˌself ˈwɜːθ/
核心：名词，不可数：自我价值感、自尊。
① Losing her job hurt her sense of self-worth, and she stopped going out with friends.
= 失业伤了她的自我价值感，她不再跟朋友出去了。
a sense of self-worth = 自我价值感
self-worth = 自我价值
② Volunteering at the hospital gave him a new feeling of self-worth after he retired.
= 退休后在医院做志愿者，让他重新找到了自身的价值。
a feeling of self-worth = 自我价值感
多和 sense、feeling 连用，心理学和日常都常见。

selfhood
/ˈselfhʊd/
核心：名词，不可数（正式）：自我、个性、自我意识。
① The poet writes about the struggle to find selfhood in a world that wants everyone to be the same.
= 那位诗人写的是在一个要求人人一样的世界里寻找自我的挣扎。
find selfhood = 寻找自我
selfhood = 自我
② For many young women in the novel, marriage means the loss of their selfhood.
= 对小说里许多年轻女子来说，结婚意味着失去自我。
the loss of selfhood = 失去自我
多见于文学、哲学写作，日常一般说 identity。

selfie
/ˈselfi/
核心：名词，可数（口语）：自拍照：take a selfie。复合词 selfie stick 是自拍杆。
① The tourists stopped on the bridge to take a selfie with the castle behind them.
= 游客们在桥上停下，以城堡为背景自拍。
take a selfie = 自拍
a selfie = 自拍照
② Selfie sticks are banned in the museum because people kept hitting the paintings with them.
= 那座博物馆禁止使用自拍杆，因为总有人用它碰到画。
a selfie stick = 自拍杆
二〇一三年前后流行开来，现在已是日常词。

selfish
/ˈselfɪʃ/
核心：形容词：自私的、只顾自己的。副词 selfishly 是自私地。名词 selfishness 另立条。
① It was selfish of him to take the last piece of cake without asking anyone else.
= 他谁都不问就拿走最后一块蛋糕，真自私。
it is selfish of somebody to do something = 某人做某事很自私
selfish = 自私的
② She selfishly kept all the money for herself and gave nothing to her brother.
= 她自私地把钱全留给自己，一分都没给她弟弟。
selfishly = 自私地
常用 it is selfish of somebody to…句型批评某个具体行为。

selfishness
/ˈselfɪʃnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：自私。
① His selfishness ruined the holiday, because he always wanted to do only what he liked.
= 他的自私毁了那次度假，因为他总是只想做自己喜欢的事。
selfishness = 自私
② The teacher spoke to the class about selfishness after some children refused to share the toys.
= 有些孩子不肯分享玩具，老师就给全班讲了自私的问题。
talk about selfishness = 谈论自私
和它相对的是 generosity（慷慨）。

selfless
/ˈselfləs/
核心：形容词：无私的、忘我的：a selfless act。副词 selflessly 是无私地。名词 selflessness 另立条。
① The nurse was praised for her selfless work during the long winter of the epidemic.
= 那位护士因在疫情漫长的冬天里忘我工作而受到表彰。
selfless work = 忘我的工作
selfless = 无私的
② He selflessly gave up his seat on the last boat so that a young mother could leave.
= 他无私地让出了最后一条船上的座位，好让一位年轻母亲离开。
selflessly = 无私地
常带赞美，用于描述为他人做出的牺牲。

selflessness
/ˈselfləsnəs/
核心：名词，不可数：无私、忘我。
① The award recognizes the selflessness of people who give their time to help others.
= 那个奖项表彰那些奉献时间帮助他人的人的无私精神。
the selflessness of somebody = 某人的无私
selflessness = 无私
② Her selflessness was clear to everyone who saw how she cared for her sick mother.
= 看过她怎样照顾生病母亲的人，都清楚她有多无私。
somebody's selflessness = 某人的无私
和 generosity 相近，更强调把别人放在自己前面。

selfsame
/ˈselfseɪm/
核心：形容词，只作定语（正式或文学）：完全相同的、同一个的：the selfsame day。用来强调 same。
① She was born and died on the selfsame day of the year, the first of May.
= 她出生和去世恰好是一年中的同一天：五月一日。
the selfsame day = 同一天
selfsame = 完全相同的
② The selfsame people who praised him last year are now calling for him to resign.
= 去年称赞他的正是如今要求他辞职的那批人。
the selfsame people = 同一批人
比 the same 语气更重，多见于书面和文学。

sell
/sel/
核心：动词（过去式和过去分词 sold）：卖、出售：sell something to somebody、sell somebody something、sell something for £10；（商品）有销路、卖得出：sell well；推销、使接受（想法）：sell an idea to somebody。反义是 buy（买）。
习语：sell like hot cakes 是畅销、抢手；sell yourself short 是低估自己、贬低自己。
短语动词与复合词：sell off 是廉价出售、变卖；sell on 是转卖；sell out 是卖光，也指（为利益）背叛原则；sell up 是（英式）卖掉全部家产；sell-through 是（零售的）售出率；selling point 是卖点；selling price 是售价。人是 seller（另立条）。
① We sold our old car to a young couple who lived in the next street.
= 我们把旧车卖给了住在隔壁街的一对年轻夫妇。
sell = 卖
sell something to somebody = 把某物卖给某人
近义对照：
buy = 买
② The little baker on the corner sells fresh bread every morning from six o'clock.
= 街角那家小面包店每天早上六点开始卖新鲜面包。
sell something = 卖某物
③ Her first novel sold well, and it was translated into twenty languages.
= 她的第一部小说卖得很好，被译成了二十种语言。
sell well = 卖得好
④ He managed to sell the idea of a four-day week to the rest of the board.
= 他成功说服董事会其他成员接受每周工作四天的想法。
sell an idea to somebody = 使某人接受某个想法
⑤ The new phones are selling like hot cakes, and most shops have run out.
= 新手机卖得像热蛋糕一样抢手，大多数商店都卖断货了。
sell like hot cakes = 畅销
⑥ Don't sell yourself short at the interview; tell them about everything you have done.
= 面试时别低估自己，把你做过的事都告诉他们。
sell yourself short = 低估自己
⑦ The company sold off its old factories in the north to pay its debts to the bank.
= 那家公司变卖了北方的旧工厂来偿还银行的债务。
sell off = 变卖
⑧ He bought the paintings cheaply and sold them on to collectors in London.
= 他低价买进那些画，再转卖给伦敦的收藏家。
sell on = 转卖
⑨ The tickets for the band's farewell concert sold out in less than ten minutes.
= 那支乐队告别演唱会的票不到十分钟就卖光了。
sell out = 卖光
⑩ Fans accused the band of selling out when they started making pop songs for adverts.
= 那支乐队开始给广告写流行歌，歌迷指责他们为钱背叛了初心。
sell out = 背叛原则
⑪ When my grandparents retired, they sold up and moved to a small town in Spain.
= 我祖父母退休时卖掉了全部家当，搬到了西班牙的一个小镇。
sell up = 卖掉全部家产
⑫ The shop measures the sell-through of each product to decide what to order next season.
= 那家店统计每种产品的售出率，决定下一季进什么货。
sell-through = 售出率
⑬ The main selling point of the flat is its view of the river.
= 那套公寓的主要卖点是能看到河景。
a selling point = 卖点
⑭ The selling price of the house was much higher than the price they had paid ten years before.
= 那座房子的售价比他们十年前买的时候高得多。
the selling price = 售价

seller
/ˈselə(r)/
核心：名词，可数：卖方、卖主、销售者；（商品）销路……的东西：a best seller、a good seller。
① The seller agreed to lower the price because the house had been on the market for a year.
